# Code-review miss corpus

Single source for the narrative corpus of bugs external review (Codex, human) caught that
`/vreview` missed. Moved here from Claude project memory on 2026-11-03 because memory does not
sync between machines. Log new rows here, never in memory. The rendered table in
`code-review-reference.html` reads `code-review-misses-data.js`; a new row goes in both, in the
four-column form of the last table. `CONTRIBUTING.md` at the repo root has the recipe.

Older rows name `detect_*.sh` detectors and `S:\repo\vlang` paths as they were at the time; the
detectors are now `detectors/detect_*.v`.

Each entry is a failing test for the review (see `/code-review` SKILL.md "Phase R").
When a reviewer finds a miss: diagnose the flaw, fix the angle as a MECHANICAL
check, then reproduce against the reviewed commit and confirm the fix clears it.
Replay this corpus to confirm the review still catches each class.

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang`, PR vlang/v#27413 (H2MuxConn), file `vlib/net/http/h2_mux_conn.v`.

| Class | Reviewed commit | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| lenient-parse `:status` | `4f5dbaa5f0` | `detect_lenient_parse.sh` | flags `:status` int() |
| lenient-parse `content-length` | `e0222e4e8c` | `detect_lenient_parse.sh` | flags line 538 u64(), exit 1 |
| error-scope (stream-window → conn fail) | `fcfa0bbf6f` | error-scope table (Angle D) | stream FC violation did `return error` not `reset_stream` |

| transport thread-safety | `c33b4372c9` | transport/external-object angle (Angle F) | reader `read()` + writer `write()` share one transport; unsafe for TLS `SSL*` (resolved by documenting the contract; TLS-safe adapter deferred to Phase 3) |
| unbounded accumulation (DoS) | `dafc8d0486` | `detect_unbounded_accum.sh` (Angle F) | `pending_ping_acks`/`pending_settings_acks` had no cap; flags NO-CAP at the reviewed commit, CAPPED after `h2_max_pending_preface_acks` |
| padding-credit gate (`data.len` vs `flow_size`) | `744325e1cf` | `detect_padding_credit.sh` (Angle E) | H2Conn + H2ServerConn wrapped `send_window_update(flow_size)` inside `if frame.data.len > 0 {}` — padding-only DATA frames (data.len==0, flow_size>0) leaked window permanently |
| lock-scope TOCTOU (`peer_max_frame` race) | `c2e1b98cad` | `detect_fmu_scope.sh` (Angle F) | `send_body_on_stream` read `peer_max_frame` under `fmu`, released `fmu` before `wmu`+DATA write — reader could process SETTINGS_MAX_FRAME_SIZE + send ACK in window → oversized frame after peer enforces new limit |
| cv-liveness (missing `fcv.broadcast()`) | `90e221f6e4` | `detect_missing_fcv_broadcast.sh` (Angle F) | fix returned excess bytes to `c.send_window` in `wmu→fmu` recheck block but omitted `fcv.broadcast()` — upload goroutines sleeping in `fcv.wait()` (window was 0) stayed blocked forever; no WINDOW_UPDATE was coming because the bytes were never sent |
| guard-completeness (`:status` fallthrough) | `90e221f6e4` | `detect_status_fallthrough.sh` (Angle A) | `all_digits(f.value)` guard added to `:status` in `H2Conn.read_response` but `got_headers = true` fired even on rejection — caller received `status == 0` with no error; malformed server response silently presented as success |
| HPACK direction (encoder vs decoder) | `37f86172ff` | Angle E (HPACK direction bullet) | `SETTINGS_HEADER_TABLE_SIZE` from server constrains our ENCODER (outgoing request headers), not our decoder; code called `c.decoder.set_max_dynamic_size()` — backwards; decoder used wrong table size when decoding response headers |
| body-completeness (truncated body) | `0aa2c8d543` | Angle E (body completeness bullet) | `wait_response` returned `resp` on `ended && got_headers` without checking `body_so_far == body_expected`; server sending END_STREAM prematurely produced a silent partial body |
| 1xx informational (headers_done latched early) | `0aa2c8d543` | Angle E (1xx informational bullet) | `on_response_headers` set `s.headers_done = true` on any HEADERS frame; a 1xx (100/103) blocked `got_headers` permanently and the final 200 HEADERS was silently treated as trailers |
| upload half-close (early final response) | `85267dd17b` | Angle E (upload half-close bullet) | `send_body_on_stream` returned cleanly on `server_ended` without RST_STREAM; server kept the stream counted against `SETTINGS_MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS` indefinitely, leaking stream slots |
| sync/mux response-validation parity (1xx + END_STREAM) | `a6884cef9d` | `detect_h2conn_1xx_end_stream.sh` (Angle C) | `H2Conn.read_response` (synchronous client) discarded a 1xx interim HEADERS frame with a bare `continue`, skipping the `if frame.end_stream { break }` handling. A 1xx carrying END_STREAM (forbidden, RFC 9113 §8.1) made read_response loop back to `next_frame()` forever waiting for a final response the stream could no longer send — a malformed server hangs the request. **The mux path `H2MuxConn.on_response_headers` already rejected this** (`!s.headers_done && frame.end_stream → reset_stream(.protocol_error)`); the sync sibling didn't. Class: **the two HTTP/2 client paths (sync `H2Conn` and mux `H2MuxConn`) must enforce the same response-validation invariants** — when one path adds a protocol check, audit the other. Codex caught it on #27413 (comment 3463064985). Fix: reject `frame.end_stream` in the 1xx branch before `continue`. Note: reviewing this fix surfaced 4 MORE parity gaps in `read_response` vs the mux path — all fixed in a follow-up parity pass (commit `f873315d97`, detector `detect_h2conn_response_parity.sh`): trailers-without-END_STREAM (hang), DATA-before-HEADERS, status `<100`/`>599`, and `101` treated as interim (hang). **Process lesson: when a reviewer flags ONE instance of a parity gap between two parallel implementations, audit the WHOLE surface of both — Codex flagged 1xx+END_STREAM, but the sync path was missing 5 of the mux path's response-validation invariants.** |
| wmu→fmu recheck scope (INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE gap) | `42da3499ee` | `detect_initial_window_recheck.sh` (Angle F) | `send_body_on_stream` wmu→fmu recheck handled `peer_max_frame` but not `s.send_window < 0`; an INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE reduction between `fmu.unlock()` and the inner `fmu.lock()` drove `s.send_window` negative → DATA sent exceeds peer's new stream window → FLOW_CONTROL_ERROR. Missed because the comment named only `peer_max_frame` as the race target; didn't enumerate ALL reader-mutable fmu-protected state. |
| pre-loop TOCTOU (first HEADERS stale max) | `42da3499ee` | `detect_headers_max_reread.sh` (Angle F) | `send_header_block_locked` CONTINUATION loop was fixed with per-iteration `fmu.lock/read/unlock` re-reads, but the first multi-frame HEADERS write still used the stale `max` snapshot from function entry. Missed by scoping the TOCTOU fix to "the loop" without checking whether the pre-loop first write had the same gap. |
| single-frame HEADERS stale max | `321e9d3920` | `detect_headers_max_reread.sh` (Angle F, updated) | `send_header_block_locked` single-frame early-return (`block.len <= max`) had no fmu re-read before its `H2HeadersFrame{` write. Detector previously checked only the second `H2HeadersFrame{` (multi-frame path), producing a false-negative: the multi-frame re-read existed, so `fmu_reads_between == 1` → OK. Updated detector checks BOTH writes independently. |
| `peer_max_frame` SETTER not serialized with writes | `f873315d97` | `detect_peer_max_frame_wmu.sh` (Angle F) | **The completion of the peer_max_frame TOCTOU family above.** All the prior fixes (`c2e1b98cad`, `42da3499ee`, `321e9d3920`) hardened the READER/sender side — re-read `peer_max_frame` under `fmu` right before the write. But a `fmu` re-read **releases `fmu` before the `wmu` write**, so it only *minimises* the window (the comments literally say "to minimise the TOCTOU window"). The residual gap stayed open because the SETTER, `apply_peer_settings`'s `h2_settings_max_frame_size` arm, updated `c.peer_max_frame` under `fmu`-**only** — so a lowered SETTINGS_MAX_FRAME_SIZE could land in the sender's `fmu`-release → write gap, emitting an oversized frame → peer FRAME_SIZE_ERROR. Codex caught it on #27413 (discussion_r3474404838). Fix: update `peer_max_frame` under `wmu` (then `fmu`) so it serializes with the senders that hold `wmu` across re-read+write, making their re-read authoritative — mirroring the `h2_settings_header_table_size` arm (already wmu-fenced). `apply_peer_settings` holds no connection lock at entry (proven: the header_table_size arm already takes wmu standalone), so wmu→fmu cannot invert. **Lesson: a recheck on the reader of shared state only closes a TOCTOU if the WRITER of that state holds the same lock the reader holds across read→use. Re-reading under lock B while the use is fenced by lock A, and the writer only takes lock B, leaves the A-fenced region racing — fix the SETTER to take lock A too.** |
| server INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE single-pass | `321e9d3920` | `detect_server_window_single_pass.sh` (Angle E/D) | `H2ServerConn.apply_settings` assigned `c.peer.initial_window_size` before the stream loop, then mutated `st.send_window` and checked for overflow — single-pass. Overflow on stream N left streams 1..N-1 inflated and the error return emitted PROTOCOL_ERROR instead of FLOW_CONTROL_ERROR. The same bug was fixed two-pass in `H2MuxConn.apply_peer_settings` in the same commit but the cross-file equivalent was missed. |
| malformed-content-length silent-ignore | `4b2bc7081e` | `detect_malformed_content_length.sh` (Angle A) | `H2Conn` parsed `Content-Length` via `all_digits(f.value)` as the outer `if` guard; a non-numeric value (e.g. `"12junk"`) silently left `has_content_length=false`, bypassing the body-length check and returning a malformed response as success. Angle A missed it when adding the over-delivery `!=` fix at line 316: should have traced back to the `has_content_length` setter at line 233 and asked "what if the value fails all_digits?" The guard is a silent-ignore; an else-error branch is required. |
| close_idle Windows double-close | `ae40710fe8` | `detect_close_idle_double_close.sh` (Angle A/B) | `close_idle` calls `net.close(handle)` (closesocket) on Windows to wake a worker blocked in `select()` (`net.shutdown(SD_BOTH)` does NOT wake it there). But the worker's `handle_conn` defer then ran `conn.shutdown()` unconditionally — a SECOND close of the same fd. Between the two closes the SOCKET value can be reused by ANY socket the process opens (Windows handle reuse is **process-wide**, not limited to this server's accept loop), so the worker's second close can land on an unrelated socket. **My earlier "fix" was itself the bug:** I added a comment claiming the double-close is safe because `ch.close()` stops the accept loop before `close_idle()` — but that only reasons about THIS server's accept loop, ignoring process-wide reuse (other servers, outbound clients, DB sockets). Codex re-flagged it on #27542 (review 4554007504). Correct fix: ownership transfer — `close_idle` records force-closed handles in a `closed []int` set (under the lock, Windows-only); the worker consults `was_force_closed(conn.handle)` and **relinquishes socket ownership (`conn.owns_socket = false`) then still calls `conn.shutdown()`** (see the follow-up leak row below — the first attempt *skipped* shutdown and leaked TLS state), so the fd is closed exactly once. **Keep the lock held across the shutdown loop**: on POSIX the worker is still the sole closer and the held lock keeps its `unmark_idle`→`net.close` ordered AFTER `close_idle`'s `net.shutdown` (dropping the lock — the other "obvious" fix — reintroduces the original POSIX fd-recycle race this PR fixed). Lesson: a "this is safe because X" comment that only covers a local scope (one accept loop) is a red flag when the resource (a SOCKET value) is global. **Shipped in vlang/v#27542 (merged 2026-06-24).** |
| close_idle force-close skips TLS cleanup (resource leak) | `31405a6150` | `detect_close_idle_double_close.sh` (Angle A, updated) | The double-close fix above made the worker **skip `conn.shutdown()` entirely** when `was_force_closed` was true (`if !was_force_closed { conn.shutdown() }`). But `SSLConn.shutdown()` does TWO jobs: free the mbedtls TLS resources (SSL ctx, config, certs, RNG, ALPN alloc) AND `net.close` the socket. Skipping it to avoid the second close also leaked all the TLS resources for every force-closed idle connection at shutdown. Codex caught it on #27542 (comment 3463041218). The miss: when gating away a call to avoid ONE of its side effects, enumerate ALL of that call's side effects — `shutdown()` was conflating socket-close with resource-free. Correct fix: split the concerns — `conn.owns_socket = false` (relinquish the socket; `shutdown` already gates `net.close` on `owns_socket`) then call `conn.shutdown()` so the TLS free always runs. Detector updated: require the worker to relinquish ownership (`owns_socket = false`) AND keep `conn.shutdown()` reachable unconditionally; flags both the double-close (no relinquish) and the leak (shutdown gated away). **Shipped in vlang/v#27542 (merged 2026-06-24).** |

| HEADERS-after-END_STREAM appended as trailers | `f541b520eb` | `detect_mux_headers_after_end.sh` (Angle D — sibling scan) | `H2MuxConn.on_response_headers` treated any second-or-later HEADERS block as trailers, gating only on `was_headers_done`. A HEADERS frame arriving after the stream already ended (a prior END_STREAM set `s.ended`) is a frame on a **closed stream** (RFC 9113 §5.1), not more trailers — with back-to-back trailer + extra HEADERS in one read buffer, the reader appends the extra fields to `resp_trailers` before `wait_response` flushes, **delivering data the peer sent after closing the stream**. **The sibling DATA path (`on_response_data`) already guarded on `s.ended`; the HEADERS path didn't** — a classic sibling-handler gap (one arm has the check, the parallel arm doesn't). Codex P2 on #27413 (comment 3482020777). Fix: early `if s.ended { reset_stream(.stream_closed); return }` after locking, before the trailers handling; legitimate trailers are unaffected (`s.ended` is false when they arrive, set afterwards). **Phase R note:** reproduced STATICALLY (detector checks for the `s.ended` guard); no deterministic runtime repro — `finish_stream` may remove the stream first and race the reader, so the post-end HEADERS could hit the unknown-stream path instead, making a runtime test a false guard. **Lesson:** when one handler arm gains a state-guard (`s.ended`), grep the sibling arms (DATA vs HEADERS vs WINDOW_UPDATE) for the same guard. Commit `dd76a2eb3b`. |

| divergent frame readers (stall path skips dispatch) | `6024da169c` | `detect_pump_delegates_dispatch.sh` (Angle C/D) | The h2 server reads frames in TWO places — `serve`→`dispatch_frame` (main loop) and `send_body`→`pump_for_window` (flow-control stall). `pump_for_window` reimplemented a SUBSET of dispatch (control frames + RST) and dropped everything else. So a frame arriving while a response was stalled on flow control — e.g. HEADERS for an over-limit stream sent before the unblocking WINDOW_UPDATE — was never HPACK-decoded (decoder desync) and the over-limit stream was never RST(REFUSED_STREAM)'d. Any rule added to dispatch_frame (PR2's refuse/idle/closed handling) silently failed to apply on the second path. Codex P2 on #27589 (discussion_r3488302384). Fix: `pump_for_window` delegates to `dispatch_frame`; enforce half-closed-remote (§5.1: DATA/HEADERS after END_STREAM → STREAM_CLOSED) which also blocks `run_request` re-entrancy; `send_body` stops gracefully if its stream is reset mid-stall (no connection teardown). **Incidentally closed h2spec 5.1.2** (over-limit stream during a stall is now refused). **Root cause (the lesson I keep relearning): when adding a rule for an event, enumerate EVERY reader of that event — grep `read_frame` callers — not just the path in front of me; a second reader that hand-picks a subset of cases is the smell. Unify the readers rather than duplicating the rule.** Detector: flags `pump_for_window` calling `read_frame` without `dispatch_frame`; exit 1 on `6024da169c`, exit 0 after fix. Commit `88a5d3435a` (#27589). |
| server stream-state: even id misclassified closed | `e97d398e02` | `detect_stream_state_even_idle.sh` (Angle E) | `H2ServerConn.classify_stream` used `id <= last_stream_id` as the sole idle/closed boundary, but `last_stream_id` tracks only client-initiated (ODD) ids. A never-opened EVEN (server-initiated) id — e.g. DATA/RST/WINDOW_UPDATE on stream 2 after the client opened odd stream 3 — was classified `.closed`, so the server sent RST(STREAM_CLOSED) for DATA / silently ignored RST/WINDOW_UPDATE instead of the required connection-level PROTOCOL_ERROR (RFC 9113 §5.1: a frame on an idle stream is a connection error). This server never opens push streams, so an even id is ALWAYS idle. Codex P2 on #27589 (discussion_r3488271404). Fix: gate `.closed` on `stream_id & 1 == 1 && stream_id <= last_stream_id`; everything else (even ids, odd ids above last_stream_id) is idle. **Phase R:** detector extracts the `classify_stream` body and flags a `return .closed` with no `& 1` parity guard — exit 1 on `e97d398e02`, exit 0 after fix. **Lesson: a stream-state classifier keyed on a monotonic counter must also test id PARITY — the counter only advances for one peer's id space (odd=client), so the "below the counter" region still contains never-opened ids of the other parity.** Commit `6024da169c` (#27589). |
| sync/mux parity: response trailers dropped | `a65240ffb0` | `detect_mux_trailers_preserved.sh` (Angle C) | A muxed response followed by a trailing HEADERS block (trailers — `grpc-status`, `digest`, ...) was HPACK-decoded by `on_response_headers` (it must, to keep decoder state in sync) but the second-HEADERS branch only set `s.ended` ("only its END_STREAM matters for this client") and **dropped every decoded field**, so `H2MuxConn` callers lost trailer metadata while the stream completed "normally". The sync `H2Conn.read_response` appends non-pseudo trailer fields to `H2ClientResponse.headers` — **same response-parity class as the 1xx+END_STREAM gap, but the OPPOSITE direction**: there the sync path was missing a check the mux path had; here the mux path was missing behaviour the sync path had. Codex P2 on #27413 (discussion_r3477245531). Fix: per-stream `resp_trailers` list — the trailers branch (`was_headers_done`) appends the non-pseudo fields, and `wait_response` flushes them into `resp.headers` under `s.mu` when the stream ends, mirroring the sync path (pseudo-header fields are forbidden in trailers and dropped). **Subtlety that shaped the fix:** trailers can NOT just be appended to `s.resp_headers` — `wait_response` snapshots that list ONCE when `headers_done` first becomes true (then sets `got_headers=true`), so a later append is silently missed; a separate list flushed at stream-end is required. **Lesson: sync/mux parity cuts BOTH ways — when two parallel implementations exist, audit what EACH path does that the other doesn't, not just newly-added checks.** Commit `dde05d7c80`. |

| H2MuxConn reader leak: optional `close_transport` | `6e5a024922` | `detect_mux_requires_closer.sh` (Angle F) | `new_h2_mux_conn(transport, close_transport)` documented `close_transport` as *optional* and defaulted it to `unsafe { nil }`. The background reader spawned in the constructor blocks in `transport.read()`; the `H2Transport` interface is read/write ONLY (no `close()` — by design, so `SSLConn` satisfies it directly via `shutdown()`), so the `close_transport` callback is the SOLE way to interrupt that blocking read at teardown. `teardown_transport` guards the call with `c.close_transport != unsafe { nil }`, so a **nil** closer makes teardown a silent no-op: an idle retirement (`shutdown_when_idle`) or last-reference `release()` leaks the reader thread AND the underlying connection forever (the reader only sets `closed` after a read error/timeout, which never arrives on a silent transport). `drop_ref`'s own comment warns of exactly this leak — but only the non-nil path avoids it. The 28 hermetic tests masked it by closing the pipe manually (`cend.close_both()`). Codex P2 on #27413 (discussion_r3475761576). Fix: **require a non-nil closer** — `new_h2_mux_conn` panics on nil before spawning the reader, so the teardown→closer→reader-exit chain is always available. (Can't add `close()` to the interface without breaking "SSLConn satisfies H2Transport directly".) Tests now pass a real closer via `new_test_mux_conn`. **Lesson:** a background thread blocked on a blocking read can ONLY be woken by closing its transport; if the close mechanism is optional/absent, teardown silently leaks the thread. Every spawned blocking reader needs a guaranteed-present wake path at teardown — make the closer required, not optional. **Phase R note:** the nil-closer leak is reproduced STATICALLY (`detect_mux_requires_closer.sh`), not at runtime: after the fix a nil closer panics, so the buggy behaviour can't be exercised in a V test (panic aborts the binary). The runtime test `test_mux_release_wakes_reader_via_closer` is a complementary guard for the teardown chain (it would be a FALSE GUARD for the nil bug — it uses a real closer that worked on the buggy commit too). |

| TLS accept-thread blocks on full channel during shutdown | `bf730df187` | `detect_tls_accept_send_interruptible.sh` (Angle A/F) | The threaded TLS server (#27552, item 3) moved the handshake to workers but enqueued each raw conn with a bare blocking `ch <- conn`. The accept loop, that send, AND the post-loop shutdown (`ch.close` → `close_idle` → `ws.wait`) all run on the ONE accept thread, while `stop()`/`close()` only flip `s.state` from another thread. Under a slow-handshake flood — every worker blocked in `complete_handshake` and the channel buffer (`pool_channel_slots`) full of untracked conns — the accept thread blocks on the send, never re-checks `s.state`, and never reaches `close_idle()`, so shutdown wedges until a worker handshake times out (≤ handshake budget). Codex P2 on #27552 (discussion_r3474019355). The PR's own comment claimed prompt stop "regardless of handshakes in flight" — too strong. Fix: enqueue through an interruptible `select` that polls `s.state` at `accept_poll_timeout`, and close the not-yet-queued conn on the accept thread (never idle-tracked → single close). Lesson: when the SAME thread both blocks on a resource (channel send) AND is responsible for the wake/cleanup (close_idle), a full resource wedges shutdown — every blocking call on a shutdown-driving thread needs a state-polling escape. |

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang`, PR vlang/v#27680 (net.quic HTTP/3 foundation), reviewed commit `a0e409eb8f`
(Codex review pullrequestreview-4781706846, 12 findings, 3 P1 / 9 P2 — the FIRST QUIC/TLS-1.3
findings in this corpus; every prior row above is HTTP/2). Both P1s are the same shape: a
security-critical check that should exist but doesn't, producing NO diff for a diff-driven angle
to react to — see the new Angle G8 added to SKILL.md as a direct result of this round.

| Class | File:line | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| missing hostname/SAN verification | `vlib/net/mbedtls/x509_standalone.v:203` (now `.c.v`) | Angle G8 (new) | `verify_certificate_chain` passed `cn: nil` always — no caller anywhere had a hostname to give it. Any trusted-CA cert for ANY hostname passed. Fixed: added mandatory `hostname string` param threaded from `Tls13ClientHandshake.server_name` through `verify_server_certificate_chain`; empty string fails closed (not treated as "skip"). Repro: standalone C-call script proved `flags` never sets `MBEDTLS_X509_BADCERT_CN_MISMATCH (0x04)` when `cn=nil`, even for an obviously-wrong hostname; new test `test_verify_certificate_chain_rejects_hostname_mismatch` asserts the flag now IS set. |
| missing ALPN (RFC 9001 §8.1 mandatory) | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_client_hello.v:202` | Angle G8 (new) / Angle E generalization | ClientHello never sent `application_layer_protocol_negotiation` at all — a real HTTP/3 server would terminate with `no_application_protocol`. Fixed: `encode_alpn_extension`/`decode_alpn_response` (RFC 7301), `ClientHelloParams.alpn_protocols` (required, non-empty), `process_encrypted_extensions` validates the server's single-entry selection is one this client offered, new `TlsAlert.no_application_protocol = 120`. 4 new tests (missing/mismatched selection, both directions). |
| transport-param connection-ID authentication gap | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_handshake.v:340` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §7.3) | `process_encrypted_extensions` checked only `initial_source_connection_id`; never validated `original_destination_connection_id` (the client's own first-Initial DCID) or `retry_source_connection_id` (Retry SCID, when present) — RFC 9000 §7.3's own anti-tampering check against an off-path attacker. Fixed: both validated, `retry_source_connection_id` also rejected when present with no Retry. 4 new tests. |
| wrong CONNECTION_CLOSE error code for transport-param failures | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_handshake.v:335` | Angle E | ALL `decode_transport_parameters` failures (structural AND its own pre-existing §18.2 bound violations) were mapped through a TLS `decode_error` alert (→ 0x132) instead of QUIC's native `TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR` (0x08, RFC 9000 §20.1) — not a TLS alert at all. Fixed: new `transport_parameter_error()` helper bypassing the `TlsAlert`/0x100+alert mapping entirely. |
| non-minimal QUIC varint wrongly rejected on decode | `vlib/net/quic/varint.v:99` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §16) | `decode_varint` rejected any value encoded in a longer-than-minimal length class — RFC 9000 §16 permits (doesn't require rejecting) any of the 4 classes a value fits in; only the ENCODER is expected to prefer minimal. A compliant peer using a longer form could never complete a handshake. Fixed: removed the rejection (encode side unaffected, still always minimal). |
| short-header spin_bit/key_phase never decoded | `vlib/net/quic/header.v:250` | Angle A (new-code line scan — struct literal omits fields) | `parse_short_header` returned `QuicShortHeader{dcid: dcid}` — `spin_bit`/`key_phase` silently stayed at their zero-value `false` regardless of the wire bits. A caller relying on `key_phase` could never detect a peer-initiated key update (RFC 9001 §6). Fixed: `buf[0] & 0x20`/`buf[0] & 0x04`. |
| QUIC v1 20-byte connection-ID limit unenforced | `vlib/net/quic/header.v:181` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §17.2) | Both `encode_long_header` and `parse_long_header` only enforced the WIRE FORMAT's own 255-byte length-field limit, not v1's own 20-byte protocol limit — a 21-255 byte CID encoded/parsed successfully despite being spec-invalid. Fixed: new `quic_v1_max_cid_len = 20` const, checked on both paths (encode conditional on `h.version == quic_v1`, parse unconditional since non-v1 versions are now rejected earlier in the same function — see next row). |
| long-header type decode not version-gated | `vlib/net/quic/header.v:98` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §17.2 vs RFC 9369 §3.2) | `peek_long_header_type` applied v1's type-bit mapping to ANY version — QUIC v2 assigns different meanings to the same 2 bits, so a v2 Initial could be misclassified as v1's zero_rtt, steering the caller to the wrong packet-number space/keys. Fixed: `parse_long_header` now rejects any non-v1 nonzero version outright before ever calling the type decoder (v2 is out of this client's committed scope entirely). |
| Retry re-derivation doc comment stated the OPPOSITE of RFC 9001 §5.2 | `vlib/net/quic/initial_secrets.v:74` | none — pure doc-only bug, would have misdirected Phase 9 implementation | The doc comment claimed Initial secrets are derived ONCE from the client's original DCID and never re-derived after a Retry. RFC 9001 §5.2's actual text: "The secrets used for constructing subsequent Initial packets change when a server sends a Retry packet to use the connection ID value selected by the server" — the OPPOSITE. Verified via a live WebFetch of the RFC text before touching anything (never trust either side of a factual RFC dispute from memory alone). No code bug (the function itself is DCID-agnostic and already correct) — but the wrong doc comment would have caused Phase 9's real QuicConn implementation to derive the wrong keys after every Retry. Fixed: rewrote the comment with the verified RFC quote, and explicitly distinguished this from the separate, unrelated `original_destination_connection_id` transport parameter (RFC 9000 §7.3 authentication, not secret derivation) — the actual source of the conflation. |
| missing 2^60 stream-count limit | `vlib/net/quic/transport_parameters.v:319` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §4.6) | Neither `initial_max_streams_bidi` nor `initial_max_streams_uni` were bounds-checked on decode; a value above 2^60 (stream ID = count × 4) can reach or exceed the 62-bit varint space. Fixed: `max_initial_max_streams = 1u64 << 60`, checked on decode (must map to TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR per the row above) AND mirrored on encode. |
| encode-side transport-parameter bounds entirely absent | `vlib/net/quic/transport_parameters.v:190` | Angle A/D (asymmetric encode/decode — one enforces bounds, the sibling doesn't) | `encode_transport_parameters` had ZERO bounds validation on any field, even though `decode_transport_parameters` already enforced `max_udp_payload_size >= 1200`, `ack_delay_exponent <= 20`, `max_ack_delay < 2^14`, `active_connection_id_limit >= 2` — `build_client_hello` forwards this struct unnormalized, so a caller configuration error produced an invalid ClientHello nobody would catch until a real server rejected it. **Lesson generalizing beyond this PR: when a decode/parse function enforces bounds, grep for the paired encode/build function on the SAME fields — an asymmetric pair (checked one direction only) is exactly this bug's shape** — mirrors this corpus's own `H2ServerConn.apply_settings` single-pass row and the sync/mux parity rows, generalized from "two parallel handlers" to "two directions of the same data". Fixed: every decode-side bound now mirrored on encode. |
| `.c.v` file-extension convention violated (AGENTS.md L313-316) | `vlib/net/mbedtls/x509_standalone.v:51` | mechanical — `grep -l 'C\.' **/*.v \| grep -v '\.c\.v$'` on changed files | A plain `.v` file directly called `C.mbedtls_x509_crt_parse`/etc. throughout — AGENTS.md's own stated rule ("Avoid using `C.`/`JS.` symbols in plain `.v` files... Use `.c.v`/`.js.v` files") was violated for an entire file, not caught because this angle didn't exist as a mechanical check yet. Fixed: `git mv` to `.c.v` (test files stay `_test.v` — that exemption is pre-existing, established repo-wide convention, confirmed via sibling `ecdsa_test.v` already doing the same). |

**Process lesson driving the SKILL.md change (Angle G8):** all 12 findings passed an earlier
`/vreview` pass on this same PR's incremental phase-by-phase diffs. The two P1s in particular
were invisible to every existing angle because G1's own ALPN/hostname check is grepped against
`SslConfig{}`/`ssl_config` construction — this PR's TLS 1.3 handshake is explicitly hand-rolled
to NEVER construct an `mbedtls_ssl_context` (that was Phase 0's own deliberate design decision),
so the grep target never existed and the angle silently didn't fire. G8 generalizes the check to
ANY standalone/hand-rolled crypto path, keyed on doc-comment language ("standalone", "does not
construct...") rather than a specific API surface.

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang-http3-foundation` (worktree of `S:\repo\vlang`), PR vlang/v#27680, reviewed
commit `5744cc62c1` (the 12-finding fix above). Codex review pullrequestreview-4782360314, a
THIRD round, 2 real P2s + 1 moot (the rsa_pss `.v`/`.c.v` convention finding, moot since that
whole module was deleted the same session as dead code) — landed AFTER a genuinely thorough
full-file holistic `/vreview` pass had already run on this PR and G8 (above) had already been
added as a direct result of the PREVIOUS round. This is the round that triggered building
`quic_conformance_matrix.md` (`.claude/skills/code-review/quic_conformance_matrix.md`) as a
standing artifact — see below.

| Class | File:line | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fixed Bit never checked on either header parser | `vlib/net/quic/header.v` `parse_long_header`/`parse_short_header` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §17.2/§17.3.1) | "Packets containing a zero value for this bit are not valid packets in this version and MUST be discarded" — never checked. A packet with Header Form set but Fixed Bit clear parsed successfully as an ordinary header. Caught AFTER a full-file holistic review of this exact file had already run. Fixed: both parsers reject a clear Fixed Bit immediately after the existing top-bit/VN checks. New tests `test_long_header_rejects_clear_fixed_bit`, `test_short_header_rejects_clear_fixed_bit`. |
| `preferred_address` connection_id length silently wraps on encode | `vlib/net/quic/transport_parameters.v` `encode_preferred_address` | Angle G3 (wire-integer overflow, silent narrowing cast) | `pa.connection_id.len` cast straight to a `u8` length byte with no bounds check — 256 bytes wraps to a length byte of 0, corrupting the wire encoding (length says 0, but 256 bytes of CID data follow, desyncing the fixed 16-byte token after it). Fixed: reject `connection_id.len > 255` before the cast, mirroring `encode_long_header`'s identical existing check. New test `test_encode_preferred_address_rejects_overlong_connection_id`. |

**Why the full-file holistic review still missed the Fixed Bit check:** Angle D (full-file
holistic) and the post-round G8 addition both ran on this file and both missed a one-sentence
RFC 9000 MUST. Root cause, diagnosed directly by the user ("So much for fixing /vreview"): Angle
E already had a *standing, maintained* conformance matrix for H2
(`h2_conformance_matrix.md`) that gets walked row-by-row — QUIC had no equivalent, only G8's
ad-hoc "write every MUST as a checklist before reading the diff" advice, which depends on
recalling every RFC 9000/9001 MUST from memory WHILE also reading the diff. That is exactly the
kind of unreliable-under-load step this same corpus's rev-16 entry already diagnosed for a
DIFFERENT reason (a checklist frame caps attention at what's already listed) — here it failed for
a second, distinct reason: nothing was written down to walk in the first place.

**Fix (methodology, not a single bug): built `quic_conformance_matrix.md`.** Mirrors
`h2_conformance_matrix.md` exactly — every RFC 9000/9001/8446/7301 normative requirement mapped
to file:line/CONFORMS/GAP for `vlib/net/quic/`'s Phase 0-2 files (varint, packet number, header,
transport parameters, initial secrets, the full TLS 1.3 handshake stack). Wired into `/vreview`'s
Angle E (`.claude/skills/vreview/SKILL.md`), replacing the ad-hoc G8 checklist note with a pointer
to the standing artifact. Building it required reading every production file line-by-line against
actual RFC text (not from memory) — the exact discipline the ad-hoc version was supposed to
provide but didn't reliably deliver under review-time pressure.

**Building the matrix immediately repeated the H2 matrix's own founding result.** The audit that
built `h2_conformance_matrix.md` (2026-06-27) found gaps G1/G2/G4 that no external review had
flagged — not a coincidence, a predictable consequence of a genuine requirements-driven read
catching absences a diff-driven read structurally cannot. The QUIC matrix's first audit did the
same on its very first run:

| Class | File:line | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| client ClientHello never sends its own mandatory `initial_source_connection_id` | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_client_hello.v` `build_client_hello` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §7.3) — found by the proactive matrix audit, NOT Codex | "An endpoint MUST treat absence of the initial_source_connection_id transport parameter from either endpoint... as a connection error." `build_client_hello` validated that four SERVER-only transport parameters were ABSENT from the client's own outgoing parameters, but never validated this client-MUST-BE-PRESENT field. Confirmed via grep: no test or production call site ever populated it on the client-send path — every ClientHello this code could produce omitted a mandatory field. Phase-R reproduced on the buggy code first (scratch script confirmed `build_client_hello` silently succeeded with the field `none`) before fixing. Fixed: same presence-check pattern as the four existing absence-checks; 7 existing test call sites updated (4 in `tls13_client_hello_test.v`, 3 in `tls13_handshake_test.v`) to supply a real value. New test `test_build_client_hello_rejects_missing_initial_source_connection_id`. |

**Still-open, explicitly acknowledged gap in the matrix itself:** the matrix only covers Phases
0-2 (what's actually on the `http3-quic-foundation` branch today) — packet protection, streams,
loss detection, and connection lifecycle exist on downstream stacked branches not yet merged
here, and will need new matrix sections (not a new file) once they land. The matrix's own "How to
use" section states this explicitly so it isn't forgotten.

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang-http3-foundation`, PR vlang/v#27680, reviewed commit `d5ba7ddfef` (the round
above). Codex review pullrequestreview-4783410111, a FOURTH round, 1 P1 + 7 P2s — triggered by a
different maintainer's `@codex review` comment (GitHub API pagination at 30 comments/page hid it
from the first check; found on a second look after the user pushed back). All 8 real, all fixed.

| Class | File:line | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| leaf certificate KeyUsage/ExtendedKeyUsage never checked (P1) | `vlib/net/mbedtls/x509_standalone.c.v` `verify_certificate_chain` / `vlib/net/quic/tls13_certificate_chain.v` `verify_server_certificate_chain` | Angle G8, extended with new checklist item (d) | Chain-of-trust + hostname alone (`mbedtls_x509_crt_verify`) say nothing about whether a leaf cert was actually ISSUED for TLS server auth — a cert restricted to clientAuth-only EKU, or missing digitalSignature KeyUsage, still authenticated. A real TLS handshake separately calls `mbedtls_ssl_check_cert_usage`; this standalone path never had an equivalent. Fixed: new `mbedtls.check_server_cert_usage`, a thin C shim delegating to mbedTLS's own unmodified `mbedtls_x509_crt_check_key_usage`/`check_extended_key_usage`, called right after chain+hostname verification succeeds. No restrictive-EKU cert fixture exists in this repo to prove the actual rejection path — test coverage is the positive path only (existing certs still accepted), stated as an explicit, unresolved limitation rather than glossed over. |
| CertificateVerify doesn't check the certificate key is the curve the SignatureScheme claims | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_certificate_chain.v` `verify_certificate_verify_signature` | Angle G8, extended with new checklist item (e) | `mbedtls_pk_verify_ext` only confirms the key is SOME EC key; `ecdsa_secp256r1_sha256` (RFC 8446 §4.2.3) names ONE specific curve (P-256). A P-384/P-521 certificate could be accepted under that scheme name even though its own signature verifies correctly on its own curve — a protocol-identity mismatch, not broken crypto. Fixed: new `mbedtls.public_key_curve_is_secp256r1` (C shim resolving `mbedtls_pk_ec`'s by-value argument + `mbedtls_ecp_keypair_get_group_id`), checked in the ecdsa branch before verification. Same fixture caveat as the row above: no non-P256 EC cert exists in this repo; proven via the closest available adjacent case (an RSA key must also fail this check, and does). |
| `decode_preferred_address`/`encode_preferred_address` missing QUIC v1's 20-byte CID limit | `vlib/net/quic/transport_parameters.v` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §17.2) | Only the wire format's 255-byte length-byte limit was enforced; the SEPARATE v1 20-byte connection-ID limit (already enforced on header.v's dcid/scid) was never applied to `preferred_address`'s connection_id on either direction. |
| Sibling CID fields (`initial_source_connection_id`/`original_destination_connection_id`/`retry_source_connection_id`) missing the SAME 20-byte limit | `vlib/net/quic/transport_parameters.v` | Angle E — **found by this session's OWN sibling sweep, not Codex**, after fixing the preferred_address instance above | Codex found ONE connection-ID field missing the limit; auditing the other three fields in the SAME file (each defined as an echo of a wire DCID/SCID already bound by the identical limit, RFC 9000 §7.3, verified via live RFC fetch) found NONE of them had it either, on either encode or decode — a sibling-search gap this session's own audit closed proactively rather than waiting for a 5th Codex round to find the 2nd, 3rd, 4th instances one at a time. |
| `encode_long_header` doesn't reject unsupported (non-v1) versions before encoding type bits | `vlib/net/quic/header.v` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §17.2 vs RFC 9369 §3.2) | `parse_long_header` already rejects any non-v1 version outright; `encode_long_header` had no equivalent, so a caller constructing a non-v1 `QuicLongHeader` would get `h.typ` silently mis-encoded under v1's bit mapping (meaningless/wrong for a version that assigns those bits differently). |
| `parse_encrypted_extensions` only rejected `early_data`, not other EE-illegal extensions | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_server_hello.v` | Angle E (RFC 8446 §4.2) | RFC 8446 §4.2: "Implementations MUST NOT send extension responses if the remote endpoint did not send the corresponding extension requests... MUST abort... with an unsupported_extension alert." `key_share`/`supported_versions` (ClientHello/ServerHello/HRR-only) passed through EncryptedExtensions unrejected. Fixed with an explicit allowlist (server_name, supported_groups, alpn, quic_transport_parameters) plus a caller-side fix so the specific `unsupported_extension` (110) QUIC error code survives instead of being unconditionally remapped to `decode_error`. |
| `parse_version_negotiation` accepts an empty versions list | `vlib/net/quic/header.v` | Angle A (degenerate/empty-input completeness, matches this codebase's own established "reject empty ALPN/cert lists" convention) | `remaining == 0` trivially passes the `% 4 != 0` check, so a VN packet advertising ZERO supported versions parsed successfully — meaningless as a negotiation offer. |
| `PROGRESS.md` still stated the OPPOSITE of the already-corrected RFC 9001 §5.2 Retry re-derivation rule | `vlib/net/quic/PROGRESS.md:67` | none — pure doc-only, second copy of a bug already fixed once in code | The PREVIOUS round (see above) fixed `initial_secrets.v`'s doc comment but never checked whether the SAME wrong claim existed anywhere else; PROGRESS.md had it too. Fixing one copy of a stated fact and not grepping for other copies is its own small instance of the sibling-search lesson. |

**Process lesson (why a matrix + a genuinely thorough full-file review still missed 8 more
things):** the CID-length and PROGRESS.md rows above are not new bug CLASSES — they're the exact
same classes as PRIOR rounds (v1's 20-byte CID limit; a doc comment stating the opposite of a
fact) recurring in SIBLING locations the earlier fix never swept. The KeyUsage/EKU and curve-check
rows ARE a new class, but one G8 was specifically built to catch — its checklist just didn't name
these two specific checks yet. Updated G8 in SKILL.md with checklist items (d) and (e); the
`quic_conformance_matrix.md` Certificate section now has explicit rows for both, so future
sibling-adjacent checks (e.g. RSA-PSS's own key-size/parameter binding, if this codebase ever adds
that scheme) have a place to land without waiting for a FIFTH round to find them one at a time.

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang-http3-foundation`, PR vlang/v#27680, reviewed commit `2ad027dbd0` (the round
above). Codex review pullrequestreview-4791164664, a FIFTH round, 1 P1 + 5 P2s. The prophecy in
this file's own prior paragraph landed almost immediately — not the SAME classes this time, but a
new pair: an allowlist fixed for ONE message type (EncryptedExtensions) never swept to its SIBLING
message types (ServerHello/HelloRetryRequest share a wire type with EncryptedExtensions's own
per-message-table concept but were never given the identical treatment), and an alert-mapping
precision gap (RFC 9001 §8.2's specifically-mandated `missing_extension` vs. the generic
`handshake_failure` this code used instead).

| Class | File:line | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| ClientHello never advertised signature_algorithms_cert (P1) | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_client_hello.v` `build_client_hello` | Angle E (RFC 8446 §4.2.3) | A server whose certificate chain is RSA-PKCS1v1.5-signed (still common among real CAs) had no signature_algorithms_cert entry to select against and could refuse the chain entirely, even though this client's own chain verification (mbedTLS's generic X.509 verifier) already supports it. `signature_algorithms` (CertificateVerify) stays strict and unchanged. Fixed: new extension listing the 4 existing schemes plus rsa_pkcs1_sha256/384/512. |
| ServerHello/HelloRetryRequest had no extension allowlist | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_server_hello.v` `parse_server_hello` | Angle E — **the exact same class of gap the EncryptedExtensions allowlist fix (prior round) closed, never extended here** | An unsolicited extension (e.g. alpn, EncryptedExtensions-only) passed through both branches silently ignored instead of the mandatory RFC 8446 §4.2 unsupported_extension abort. Fixed with variant-specific allowlists + the same error-code-preservation fix applied to the caller. **Lesson: fixing an allowlist for one TLS message type doesn't imply the sibling message types got it — check every message type sharing the same "which extensions are legal here" concept, not just the one Codex named.** |
| Missing quic_transport_parameters mapped to handshake_failure instead of missing_extension | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_handshake.v` `process_encrypted_extensions` | Angle E (RFC 9001 §8.2) | RFC 9001 §8.2's exact text: "MUST close the connection with an error of type 0x016d (equivalent to a fatal TLS missing_extension alert)." Code used generic handshake_failure (0x128) instead. Fixed: new TlsAlert.missing_extension = 109 (IANA-confirmed). |
| stateless_reset_token/preferred_address accepted alongside a zero-length server connection ID | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_handshake.v` `process_encrypted_extensions` | Angle E (RFC 9000, cross-field) | Neither decoder can see this cross-field inconsistency alone; only the caller holding peer_initial_scid can. Fixed with an explicit check. Note: the exact RFC 9000 §18.2 defining paragraphs for these two parameters could not be independently re-verified via WebFetch during this round (the tool consistently truncated before reaching that section of a very long RFC) — fixed based on Codex's precise citation plus corroborating adjacent text, stated honestly rather than presented as independently re-verified. |
| README.md still described the deleted crypto.rsa_pss module as a hard OpenSSL dependency | `vlib/net/quic/README.md` | Angle D (doc-only, stale after an earlier round's code change) | Same class as the PROGRESS.md miss two rounds ago: a doc file not updated when the code it described changed. |
| encode_packet_number silently truncates exhausted packet numbers instead of rejecting them | `vlib/net/quic/packet_number.v` | Angle A/G3 (wire-integer silent narrowing, infallible function with no error path) | full_pn >= 2^62 (RFC 9000 §12.3's own limit) truncates to a 4-byte all-zero field. Fixed: signature changed from infallible to fallible (no production caller existed yet, so a clean change), rejects before the sizing arithmetic. |

**Process lesson:** two of six are the SAME shape as this file's own preceding "process lesson"
paragraph predicted before this round even landed — a fix scoped to the one message type or one
comment Codex named, not swept to its siblings. The other four are new classes (alert-mapping
precision, a genuinely new missing extension, a cross-field validation gap, an infallible-function
correctness gap) that no existing angle bullet named yet before this round.

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang-http3-foundation`, PR vlang/v#27680, reviewed commit `d263f9195f` (a small
CI-fix commit, not a Codex round itself). Codex review pullrequestreview-4806500473, a SIXTH round,
1 P3 + 8 P2s, fixed in commit `393b1dcd8c`. Two findings were NOT accepted at face value — verified
independently and found to be partially, not fully, correct, logged as such rather than silently
"fixed and moved on."

| Class | File:line | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| `parse_short_header` panics instead of erroring on a negative `dcid_len` | `vlib/net/quic/header.v` | Angle A (guard completeness — a `!`-declared function reaching a panic instead of its own error path) | `buf.len < 1 + dcid_len` doesn't reject when `dcid_len` is negative (both sides go negative together), so the code reaches `buf[1..1 + dcid_len]`, a slice with `start > end`. Reproduced before fixing: `parse_short_header([]u8{len: 10, init: 0x40}, -1)` crashed with `V panic: array.slice: invalid slice index (start>end):1, 0` — a genuine Phase-R crash repro, not just a reasoned-through claim. Fixed with an explicit `dcid_len < 0` guard. |
| `signature_algorithms_cert` missing P-384/P-521 ECDSA cert-signing schemes | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_client_hello.v` `encode_signature_algorithms_cert_extension` | Angle E (RFC 8446 §4.2.3, sibling to the rev-25/round-5 RSA-PKCS1 fix) | Same class as round 5's P1 (signature_algorithms_cert missing schemes this client can actually verify), different algorithm family this time. Verified the vendored mbedTLS build actually supports both curves BEFORE adding them (`MBEDTLS_ECP_DP_SECP384R1_ENABLED`/`SECP521R1_ENABLED` + `MBEDTLS_SHA512_C`, confirmed in `mbedtls_config.h`) — a claim this specific could have been wrong, and checking it first is what round 5's own reply-thread precedent established as the right discipline. |
| CertificateVerify unoffered-algorithm alert misclassified as decode_error | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_certificate.v` `parse_certificate_verify` / `tls13_handshake.v` `process_certificate_verify` | Angle A (error-code dropped across a caller boundary — the SAME pattern as `process_server_hello`/`process_encrypted_extensions`, now a THIRD instance) | RFC 8446 §4.4.3 states the algorithm MUST be one offered but does NOT itself name an alert for the violation (verified: fetched the section directly, the word `illegal_parameter` does not appear there). **Citation precision matters independently of whether the underlying finding is right** — the classification chosen (`illegal_parameter`) is well-corroborated (RFC 8446 §6.2's general definition, this file's own established cipher_suite-not-offered precedent, and majority real-world implementation practice: OpenSSL/BoringSSL/GnuTLS/s2n/wolfSSL), but the PR reply and this row say so explicitly rather than implying the exact quote exists when it doesn't. |
| `encode_packet_number`'s 4-byte length-selection arm has no upper bound | `vlib/net/quic/packet_number.v` | Angle G3 (wire-integer silent narrowing) — a SECOND, INDEPENDENT bound on the same repeat-offender function, distinct from round 5's `max_packet_number` check | Caps `full_pn` itself; this new bound caps the GAP relative to `largest_acked`, independently reachable even for a small `full_pn` given a stale/absent `largest_acked`. Reproduced before fixing: `encode_packet_number(max_packet_number, Some(0))` produced 4 bytes that `decode_packet_number` reconstructed as `4294967295` (2^32-1), not the `4611686018427387903` (2^62-1) actually sent — a genuine round-trip corruption, not a theoretical one. **Repeat-offender note:** this function was ALREADY touched in round 5 for a DIFFERENT bound on the SAME arithmetic; re-derived its full contract from scratch per the standing rule rather than treating the new check as an isolated patch, and confirmed the two bounds are logically independent (neither implies the other). |
| HelloRetryRequest treated `key_share` as mandatory | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_server_hello.v` `parse_server_hello` | Angle E (RFC 8446 §4.1.4, direct fetch: "The server's extensions MUST contain 'supported_versions'" — key_share not named as mandatory) | Rejected a valid cookie-only HRR (client's already-offered share acceptable, server wants only an anti-DoS cookie round-trip) before it could ever be represented as a `ParsedHelloRetryRequest`. Fixed: `selected_group` is now `?u16`; the one downstream consumer (`process_server_hello`) updated to only validate the group when present. |
| `server_name` extension in EncryptedExtensions not required to have empty `extension_data` | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_server_hello.v` `parse_encrypted_extensions` | Angle E (RFC 6066 §3, direct fetch confirmed verbatim: "the server SHALL include an extension of type 'server_name' ... SHALL be empty") | A non-empty payload passed through silently. Fixed with an explicit length check when the extension is present. |
| `preferred_address` IPv4/IPv6 address+port pairs not checked for internal consistency | `vlib/net/quic/transport_parameters.v` `encode_preferred_address`/`decode_preferred_address` | Angle E (RFC 9000 §18.2) — **not a confirmed literal MUST, marked 🔸 defensive like this file's own duplicate-parameter-ID precedent** | §18.2 defines all-zero address+port TOGETHER as "not provided"; the RFC text does not appear to explicitly define a MISMATCHED pair. Fixed defensively on both encode and decode (an asymmetric one-sided fix is exactly this file's own previously-corrected bug shape). |
| ServerNameList/client_shares length bounds ignore their own wrapping overhead | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_client_hello.v` `encode_server_name_extension`/`encode_key_share_extension` | Angle A/G3 — **PARTIALLY CONFIRMED, not accepted at face value**: the bound was genuinely wrong, but the claimed CONSEQUENCE was empirically refuted | The finding claimed a hostname of 65533-65535 bytes "silently succeeds" and "produces a malformed ClientHello instead of returning an error." Traced the arithmetic: `data.len` (the OUTER TLS extension_data, checked generically by `encode_extension`) exceeds 65535 for name_bytes.len ≥ 65531 — BEFORE the claimed 65533 threshold — so `encode_extension`'s own existing generic guard already rejects every value the too-loose bound let through, just with a misattributed "extension data too large" message instead of naming the hostname. Confirmed via a direct test run against the un-fixed code: it returned an error, not a success, for the exact value named in the finding. Fixed anyway (tightened the bound to the correct value, `0xffff - 5`, for a correctly-attributed message at the right layer) and found the identical class of gap in the sibling `encode_key_share_extension` proactively. **Class: a reviewer's citation of a REQUIREMENT being wrong does not automatically make their claimed CONSEQUENCE true — trace the actual downstream guards before accepting "no error was returned" as a given.** |
| `PROGRESS.md` still marked the removed `rsa_pss` module as complete | `vlib/net/quic/PROGRESS.md` | Angle D (doc-only, stale after an earlier round's code removal) | Same class as the README.md miss from two rounds ago — a doc file not updated when the code it described changed, recurring a second time in a DIFFERENT doc file for the SAME underlying removal. |

**Process lesson (why two findings needed independent verification, not just a fix):** this round's
two most interesting rows aren't bugs at all in the usual sense — they're findings whose CITATION
or CONSEQUENCE needed checking on its own, separately from whether "something here is wrong." The
CertificateVerify alert finding's underlying claim (needs a more specific alert than decode_error)
is right, but the specific RFC section named doesn't say what was implied. The ServerNameList
finding's underlying claim (the bound is wrong) is right, but the specific consequence named
(silent corruption) doesn't happen, because an unrelated guard already catches it. Both were fixed
anyway (the code changes are correct improvements either way), but the PR replies and this corpus
say precisely what was and wasn't independently verified, rather than blanket-accepting a
plausible-sounding claim because the underlying direction was correct. This is the same discipline
this session already applies to its OWN claims (see the Graphify/re-aliasing distinction logged
earlier the same week) — apply it symmetrically to an external reviewer's claims too.

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang-http3-foundation`, PR vlang/v#27680, reviewed commit `0c5be27ef7` (a merge of
`upstream/master` into the branch to clear unrelated CI noise, not a Codex round itself). Codex
review pullrequestreview-4822597219, a SEVENTH round, 6 P2s, fixed in commits `47f59746ad`
(rename-only) + `8aa4d6b6af` (logic fixes). One finding REFUTED after a direct RFC fetch; one
finding's specific claimed CONSEQUENCE ("`-Wimpure-v` reports this") empirically checked and found
NOT to hold, though the underlying fix (matching AGENTS.md's written rule + a sibling precedent)
still landed; and — the first of its kind in this corpus — a bug this session INTRODUCED WHILE
FIXING a Codex finding, caught by its OWN `/vreview` pass before push, not by Codex or a human.

| Class | File:line | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| `encode_packet_number`'s no-ack branch never checked `full_pn` fits in the 4-byte encoding it always chooses | `vlib/net/quic/packet_number.v` | Angle G3 (wire-integer silent narrowing) — a THIRD, INDEPENDENT bound on the same repeat-offender function (rounds 5 and 6 each added a different one) | `num_unacked` in this branch is a fixed sentinel (`0x7FFF_FFFF`), never derived from `full_pn`, so round 6's gap check (`(num_unacked+1)*2 > 2^32`) was permanently false here regardless of `full_pn`'s actual size — a check that LOOKS like it covers this branch but structurally cannot. Reproduced before fixing: `encode_packet_number(0x1_0000_0000, none)` returned 4 zero bytes; decode reconstructed `0`, not `4294967296`. **Repeat-offender note:** re-derived the full contract from scratch again (third time this function has been touched); confirmed this bound is independent of both prior ones (neither the `max_packet_number` cap nor the gap-vs-`largest_acked` cap implies "does full_pn fit in 4 bytes when there's no reference point at all"). |
| `parse_certificate` stored each CertificateEntry's extensions with no legality check at all | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_certificate.v` `parse_certificate` | Angle E (RFC 8446 §4.2's applicability table, direct fetch) | §4.2's table permits only `status_request`/`signed_certificate_timestamp` in a CertificateEntry ("CT"); `supported_versions` (Codex's example) is CH/SH/HRR-only. Checked further than the citation itself: this client offers NEITHER of the two CT-legal extensions, so the correct legal set is empty, not just "block supported_versions specifically" — a broader, more complete fix than the literal finding asked for. Reproduced before fixing: a synthetic Certificate message with a `supported_versions` CertificateEntry extension parsed with no error. |
| **Self-caught, not a Codex finding: fixing the row above INTRODUCED a code-discard bug, caught by this round's own `/vreview` pass before push** | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_handshake.v` `process_certificate_or_request` | Angle C (cross-file caller/callee trace) — checking the NEW error-code-carrying error's caller, not just the callee that produces it | `parse_certificate`'s new `error_with_code(..., unsupported_extension)` was unconditionally remapped to a generic `decode_error` by its only caller, discarding the classification entirely — the exact `err.code() != 0` preservation pattern already used by 3 OTHER call sites in the SAME file (`process_server_hello`/`process_encrypted_extensions`/`process_certificate_verify`), just never applied to this fourth one because it hadn't needed a code-carrying error before this round. Reproduced before fixing: driving a real handshake through to a tampered Certificate message and checking `err.code()` directly returned `306` (decode_error's code), not the expected `unsupported_extension` code. **Process lesson: Angle C's cross-file caller check must be re-run for a NEW error path you just added, not only for pre-existing ones — writing a code-carrying error and never checking whether its caller actually threads that code through is exactly the same shape as the miss this angle already exists to catch, just self-inflicted instead of externally reported.** |
| `supported_groups` extension's inner NamedGroupList framing never validated | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_server_hello.v` `parse_encrypted_extensions` | Angle E (RFC 8446 §4.2.7 / RFC 7919) | Only the outer extension TLV framing was checked (by the shared `parse_extension_list`); the inner 2-byte length prefix was never checked against the bytes actually present. Reproduced before fixing: extension_data `[0x00, 0x02, 0xAA]` (inner length claims 2 bytes, 1 present) parsed with no error. This client never reads this field's value at all (key exchange goes through `key_share`), so the practical impact is limited to accepting a malformed opaque value — still fixed, since correctness of a structural parse shouldn't depend on whether the parsed value is later consumed. |
| ALPN protocol names required to be unique | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_client_hello.v` `encode_alpn_extension` | **REFUTED as an RFC requirement — then implemented anyway on maintainer request** — fetched RFC 7301 §3.1 directly | The cited requirement doesn't exist: §3.1 states only that empty/truncated strings are forbidden, nothing about uniqueness. `protocols` is caller-supplied (so a duplicate is reachable), but a duplicate creates no wire-format or protocol ambiguity — kept off the baseline in round 7, no code change at the time. A maintainer (JalonSolov) then followed up on the thread agreeing with the RFC read but requesting the check anyway as hygiene (a duplicate wastes wire bytes for no negotiation benefit) — landed in vlang/v#28001 (separate PR, since #27680 had already merged by then): `encode_alpn_extension` now tracks seen names via a `map[string]bool` and rejects a repeat. **Lesson: a REFUTED citation is not necessarily the end of the story — a maintainer can still want the behavior for reasons the RFC doesn't cover, and disagreeing with a reviewer's CITATION doesn't mean disagreeing with their SUGGESTED FIX is the only valid outcome.** |
| `tls13_certificate_chain.v` embeds `C.mbedtls_x509_crt` in a plain `.v` file | `vlib/net/quic/tls13_certificate_chain.v` (renamed to `.c.v`) | AGENTS.md L313-316 (C/JS interop hygiene) — **claimed mechanism checked and found NOT to hold, fix still applied** | Codex claimed `-Wimpure-v` currently reports this file as impure. Built `net/quic` with `-Wimpure-v` directly: it does NOT flag this file — the warning only fires for `C.` in VALUE position (`const x = C.SOME_CONST`), not TYPE position (`chain &C.mbedtls_x509_crt`), which is this file's only usage; this repo's CI doesn't run `-Wimpure-v` at all either. The underlying suggestion still stands independent of the tool: AGENTS.md's written rule doesn't condition on what the current build catches, and `net.mbedtls`'s own `x509_standalone.c.v` already keeps every such usage in a `.c.v` file — this was the only file in `net/quic` breaking that established sibling pattern. Renamed (pure rename, no logic change). |

**Process lesson (the self-caught finding is the first of its kind in this corpus):** every prior
round's rows are either "Codex found it" or "Codex's citation/consequence needed independent
verification." This round adds a third shape: a bug this session's OWN fix introduced, caught by
this session's OWN `/vreview` pass on the resulting diff, before anything was pushed or reviewed
externally. It was caught specifically because Angle C ("for each changed function, grep for its
callers... does the change break a precondition, return shape...") was applied to the NEWLY-WRITTEN
`error_with_code` call, not just to pre-existing code — the same angle that would have caught this
as an external miss if it had shipped. Lesson: after adding ANY `error_with_code`/similar
classified-error call, immediately grep every caller of the function you just changed and confirm
at least one of them actually threads the new code through — don't assume "I followed the
established pattern" without checking the SPECIFIC caller in front of you.

# Behavioural repro: test_server_tls_close_under_handshake_flood in server_tls_test.v

**Phase R testing lesson (TLS handshake-flood repro — 3 false guards before it caught the bug):**
Building this regression test took THREE failed attempts that all PASSED on the buggy code
(false guards). Root causes, each only found by forcing the assert to fail and printing the real
close time + instrumenting the accept loop:
1. **Bare silent `dial_tcp` doesn't stall the handshake** — mbedtls fails the empty/EOF read FAST
   (fd reuse in the trace proved workers were draining, not blocking). Need a *valid* ClientHello
   so the server accepts it and then blocks in `wait_for_read` for the client's next flight.
   Captured a real 165-byte ClientHello from V's own mbedtls client and replayed it.
2. **`tls_accept_timeouts` derives the handshake budget from `accept_timeout` when finite** — so
   `accept_timeout: 100ms` capped each stuck handshake to 100ms (NOT the `tls_handshake_timeout`
   fallback, which only applies when `accept_timeout` is 0/infinite). Workers freed in 100ms → no
   wedge. Fix: set `accept_timeout: 8s` so the budget is large.
3. **Timing/backlog** — dialing with gaps + closing too soon left the extra conns in the TCP
   backlog un-accepted, so the accept thread was polling (not blocked on the send). Dial all conns
   up front, then sleep so the accept thread fills worker+buffer and parks on the send.
Meta-lesson: a shutdown/liveness test that PASSES on the known-buggy commit is a FALSE GUARD —
Phase R ("reproduce on the buggy commit, exit 1") is the only thing that catches it. Always
force the assert to fail once to read the real measured value before trusting a timing assertion.

| GOAWAY admission-flag write-race | `4496950803` | `detect_goaway_wmu_serialize.sh` (Angle A/F) | `do_on_stream` rechecks the terminal admission flags (`closed`/`goaway`/`shutting_down`) under `smu` *while holding `wmu`*, then releases `smu` and writes the request HEADERS still under `wmu`. The `H2GoawayFrame` handler set `c.goaway = true` under `smu` **only** — so a GOAWAY landing between that recheck (smu released after registration) and the HEADERS write let the client open one more stream after observing GOAWAY (RFC 7540 §6.8: "Receivers of a GOAWAY frame MUST NOT open additional streams"). Angle F enumerated reader-mutable `fmu`-protected state for earlier TOCTOU fixes but never asked the parallel question for `smu`-protected admission flags: which terminal-flag *setters* fail to serialize with the `wmu` write section that rechecks them? Fix: take `wmu` before `smu` (the permitted nesting) in the GOAWAY handler so `c.goaway` cannot be set while any writer holds `wmu`; the recheck becomes authoritative. Deadlock-free: the reader already acquires `wmu` in the SETTINGS/PING ACK paths (and there holds it across a blocking write), so this adds no new property — and the GOAWAY arm only sets a flag under `wmu`. (`closed` is self-healing — `fail_conn` implies transport death so the write fails anyway; `shutting_down` via `shutdown_when_idle` has the same residual gap but is benign — the slipped-in stream completes normally.) |
| cross-file empty-arm (HPACK table size) | `5be13c6431` | `detect_settings_arm_parity.sh` (Angle C) | `H2MuxConn.apply_peer_settings` had a comment-only `h2_settings_header_table_size` arm asserting "nothing to constrain"; `H2Conn.apply_settings` and `H2ServerConn.apply_settings` both set `encoder.dyn_table.set_max_size` + `encoder.pending_max_table_size` for the same arm. RFC 7541 §6.3 requires a Dynamic Table Size Update prefix on the next HEADERS block even when the encoder uses only literals. Angle C traced callers/callees but did not compare match arms across sibling functions in parallel files. Fix: add `c.wmu.lock(); c.encoder.dyn_table.set_max_size(int(st.value)); c.encoder.pending_max_table_size = int(st.value); c.wmu.unlock()` under the header_table_size arm. General detector `detect_settings_arm_parity.sh` flags any `h2_settings_*` arm that is comment-only in the target file but has executable code in any sibling `h2_*.v`. |

The one detector now enforces the full invariant: the worker must relinquish
socket ownership (`owns_socket = false`) on force-close AND keep `conn.shutdown()`
reachable unconditionally. Exit 1 covers both failure modes — no relinquish
(double-close) and `shutdown()` gated away (TLS leak). Supersedes
`detect_close_idle_windows_double_close.sh` (which only checked `net.close`
presence — necessary but not sufficient).
