Fixed by adding an explicit step 0 to "How to use": grep `⏳` and cross-check against `git log` for every changed file BEFORE using the matrix to argue "already covered," run at the start of every Codex-round triage, not just when directly editing a listed file. **Repro:** `grep -n "⏳" .claude/skills/code-review/quic_conformance_matrix.md` then for each hit, `git log --oneline <base>..HEAD -- <cited-file>` -- any non-empty result on this branch means the row is stale. |

| Deferred-state doc comment understated its own RFC requirement | `vlib/net/quic/retry.v` `verify_retry_integrity_tag` | none (mirrors an existing sibling pattern, not a new mechanical check) | Codex round 7 on PR vlang/v#27880 (pullrequestreview-4840201604): the function's own doc comment said RFC 9000 §17.2.5.2 requires discarding only "a second Retry" -- the actual second sentence is broader ("after processing an Initial OR Retry, discard any subsequent Retry"), so a correctly-tagged Retry arriving after the client had already processed the server's Initial still passed both `parse_retry_packet` and this verifier, since neither received that connection state at all. Fixed by adding `already_processed_other_packet bool`, checked before tag verification -- an exact mirror of `version_negotiation.v`'s existing `already_processed_other_packet` parameter for the analogous RFC 9000 §6.2 VN-discard requirement (finding F1/P2, same PR, earlier round) -- rather than re-deferring with just a corrected comment. Two files in the SAME PR had handled structurally identical "discard once other connection state exists" requirements differently (VN: implemented; Retry: deferred-and-under-documented) until this fix brought them to parity. `parse_retry_packet`'s doc comment also gained an explicit calling-order contract (verify first, only parse what verify accepts) it previously left unstated. Regression test proves the discard is attributable to the flag, not an incidental tag mismatch: the same packet verifies true with the flag false, false with it true. |
| Wire-integer overflow: offset+length addition wraps for an offset near u64::MAX | `vlib/net/quic/crypto_stream.v` `CryptoStreamReassembler.add` | Angle G3 (wire-integer arithmetic overflow) | Codex round 7 on PR vlang/v#27880 (pullrequestreview-4840201604): `end := offset + u64(data.len)` wraps to a small value when `offset` is near `u64::MAX`, silently passing the `end > max_crypto_stream_buffered_bytes` guard computed from the wrapped result -- the exact "guard sees the wrapped result and incorrectly accepts it" pattern Angle G3 already names, but the angle wasn't run against this specific `pub fn` since its only current production caller (`frame.v`'s `parse_crypto_frame`) already bounds `offset+length <= 2^62-1` upstream, making the check LOOK unnecessary from a diff-only read. Fixed by rejecting on `offset` alone before computing the sum, which also makes the addition provably overflow-free afterward. Phase-R'd: `r.add(max_u64, [1]u8)` succeeded pre-fix, rejects post-fix. **Lesson: an upstream caller already bounding a value does not exempt a `pub fn`'s OWN stated contract from defending itself independently** -- this function's own doc comment for `max_crypto_stream_buffered_bytes` explicitly frames the bound as the standalone defense against "a malicious or buggy peer," not as something contingent on what today's one caller happens to already check. **Repro:** `r.add(max_u64, [u8(0x41)])` -- succeeds on the pre-fix code, rejects with a "buffering limit" error after. |

| A prior round's OWN fix regressed a legitimate case, because a requirement's literal subject was read out of its role-scope | `vlib/net/quic/coalesce.v` `split_coalesced_datagram` | none -- required checking the requirement's applicable ROLE against what this codebase implements, a judgment call not a syntactic pattern | Codex round 8 on PR vlang/v#27880 (pullrequestreview-4843018164): round 6 (pullrequestreview-4839675790) added an unconditional discard of any Initial packet in a datagram under RFC 9000 §14.1's 1200-byte floor, citing "A server MUST discard an Initial packet..." -- but that MUST binds a SERVER receiving an undersized CLIENT Initial (anti-amplification), and a server's OWN Initial is only required to be padded when it's ACK-ELICITING (RFC 9000 Table 3 marks ACK/PADDING/CONNECTION_CLOSE "N", not ack-eliciting) -- so a legitimate server response containing only e.g. an ACK or an early CONNECTION_CLOSE can be smaller than 1200 bytes, and round 6's fix discarded it. **This is the SAME "citation and consequence are two separate things" discipline already in `/codex-respond` Phase 2, just missed on my OWN prior fix rather than on a reviewer's finding** -- I verified the 1200-byte NUMBER and the general shape of the requirement against the primary RFC text (confirmed via a real captured server datagram already at exactly 1200 bytes), but never checked WHO the "MUST discard" bound (server, not any receiver) or under what CONDITION the sender-side pairing requirement applied (ack-eliciting only, not unconditionally) -- reading a requirement's literal sentence is not the same as reading its full applicable scope. Reverted entirely (not narrowed): `split_coalesced_datagram` operates on still-AEAD-protected bytes with no visibility into whether a payload is ack-eliciting, and this codebase is client-only (Phase 13/server explicitly deferred) so the server-side half of the check has no role to attach to yet -- the real requirement needs a later, role-aware, post-decryption layer. Reverted the 3 test-padding additions and the dedicated regression test from round 6 back to original form. Also corrected the `quic_conformance_matrix.md` row this same finding had created in round 6 (previously marked erroneously ✅) to N/A (out of scope), with the two-round history kept as a cautionary example in the row itself. **Repro:** a `QuicLongHeader{typ: .initial, length: 2}` encoded + 2 payload bytes (representing e.g. an ACK-only Initial, well under 1200 bytes) -- `split_coalesced_datagram` returned 0 packets on the round-6 code, 1 on the reverted code. |
| Function's own doc comment stated a bool contract; one branch still errored, contradicting it | `vlib/net/quic/retry.v` `verify_retry_integrity_tag` | none (an internal self-consistency check the "read your own doc comment as a checklist" discipline should have caught, but didn't, on the SAME round that wrote the doc comment) | Codex round 8 on PR vlang/v#27880 (pullrequestreview-4843018164), P1: round 7 (pullrequestreview-4840201604) rewrote this function's doc comment to say "FALSE means discard it... TRUE means..." -- a strict bool contract -- while adding a NEW bool-returning early-return (`already_processed_other_packet`), but left the PRE-EXISTING too-short-packet branch as `return error(...)`, unnoticed, in the SAME edit that added the doc comment describing the contract that branch violates. A caller propagating `!` on that error would treat an unverifiable (not merely invalid-tag) Retry as connection-fatal, when RFC 9000 §17.2.5.2 requires discarding it silently, identically to a tag mismatch. Fixed: the too-short branch now returns `false`. **Root cause, precisely named:** the "read your own doc comment as a checklist against your own code" discipline (established after round 3's citation-shaped-fix miss on this SAME PR) was applied to the NEW code being written that round, but not swept across the REST of the function's pre-existing body in the same edit -- the repeat-offender full-contract-re-derivation rule (Phase 0) should have caught this, since this function was touched again in the SAME round it was supposed to protect against exactly this. Updated the one existing test asserting the old error behavior to assert `false`. **Repro:** `verify_retry_integrity_tag([u8(1)], [u8(1),2,3], false)` returned an error on the round-7 code, `false` (no error) after the fix. |

| Skill tool served a stale, pre-edit cached copy of SKILL.md mid-session | n/a -- tooling/process gap, not a code bug | none (no way found to force-refresh from within a session) | The user directly edited `.claude/skills/vreview/SKILL.md` on disk mid-session (a 10-revision process-improvement pass: Review Modes, a new Angle H, required-artifacts blocks, a 6-column sibling parity table, 3 concurrency matrices), synced into `code-review-reference.html` up through rev 41. Invoking the `Skill` tool for `/vreview` AFTER those edits still returned the OLD content verbatim (no Angle H, no Review Modes, still "A-G") -- confirmed by reading the actual file on disk (`grep -n "Angle H" .claude/skills/vreview/SKILL.md` found it at line 1502; the Skill tool's returned text had no such section). The user's own question ("Why no /vreview") surfaced this -- I had run an ad-hoc inline equivalent (contract re-derivation, detector suite, manual Phase M marker) instead of actually invoking the skill, and when I DID invoke it to comply, it silently served stale instructions rather than erroring or refreshing. **Practical fix used this session:** read the SKILL.md file directly with the Read tool instead of trusting the Skill tool's returned content, then manually applied the CURRENT process (Phase 0's Review Mode selection, the expanded checklist, Angle H's absence-search, the full Artifacts block) by hand. **Open question, not yet answered:** whether every `/vreview` invocation in THIS session after the user's edits landed was silently reviewing against the stale pre-edit process (rounds 6/7's `/vreview` calls may have missed Review Mode selection, Angle H, and the newer required-artifacts discipline entirely) -- flagged to the user rather than assumed either way. |

| New function's doc comment omitted a compositional detail every actual caller already relied on | `vlib/net/quic/coalesce.v` `pad_initial_payload` | none -- requires explicitly diffing a doc comment's stated parameter semantics against real call-site expressions, a check no existing angle names | GitHub Copilot review on PR vlang/v#27880 (pullrequestreview-4875246955): `pad_initial_payload(payload, header_len, aead_tag_len)`'s doc comment described `header_len` only as "bytes of already-built header," never stating it must include the packet-number field length -- yet every call site (`initial_exchange_test.v`) already passes `encode_long_header(...).len + pn_length`, not the bare header length. The function's OWN body is unaffected (it only ever sums `header_len + payload.len + aead_tag_len` against the 1200-byte floor) -- a future caller who read only the doc comment and passed the bare header length would silently under-pad by 1-4 bytes below the RFC 9000 §14.1 floor, with no test catching it since every EXISTING caller already (correctly, but undocumented-ly) includes the PN bytes. **Root cause, precisely named:** this function was itself written in a round-6 FIX commit (`75fd97f54d`, responding to a different Codex finding: raw-trailing-bytes padding should be PADDING frames). Fix-commit verification checks "does this satisfy the specific finding," not "does this new function's doc comment fully specify its calling contract" -- the repeat-offender full-contract-re-derivation rule never fired because `pad_initial_payload` itself was touched exactly once (rounds 7/8 both touched a DIFFERENT function, `split_coalesced_datagram`, in the same file, so per-function repeat-offender tracking never flagged it). No existing vreview angle asks "for each documented parameter, does the doc's stated semantics match what real call sites actually pass" -- this is distinct from Angle A's line-by-line correctness scan (the code IS correct) and from Angle H's absence search (a spec requirement, not a doc-completeness gap). **Repro:** none in the traditional Phase-R sense -- no runtime behavior differs; the fix is a doc-only clarification. Fixed in `e25ebddc27`. |
| Existing test predated a later doc-comment contract tightening and was never swept for compliance | `vlib/net/quic/retry_test.v` `test_retry_packet_round_trip_verifies` | none -- requires re-checking EXISTING callers/tests against a contract that changed in a LATER commit, not just the touched function's own body | GitHub Copilot review on PR vlang/v#27880 (pullrequestreview-4875246955): this test called `parse_retry_packet` before `verify_retry_integrity_tag`, contradicting `parse_retry_packet`'s own doc comment ("Callers MUST call verify_retry_integrity_tag first and only call this function when it returns true"). The test itself was written on day one of Phase 4 (`4f3c724c61`), BEFORE that MUST-verify-first sentence existed -- the sentence was added later, in round 7 (`7f434ed2ad`), as a doc-only clarification alongside the `already_processed_other_packet` fix. Round 8 subsequently re-derived `verify_retry_integrity_tag`'s FULL contract from scratch per the repeat-offender rule and checked it against the function's OWN body (correctly catching that round's real P1: the too-short-packet branch still `return error(...)`'d) -- but that re-derivation never widened to grep the function's EXISTING callers/tests for compliance with the newly-stated contract. The test still passed either way (its packet comes from the test's own fixture builder, not untrusted input), so no functional-behavior check would ever surface this -- only a review that diffs doc prose against call-site code catches it. **Root cause, precisely named:** the "impact analysis before narrowing/changing EXISTING behavior" rule (`/codex-respond` Phase 4) only triggers on BEHAVIORAL changes; a doc-only contract clarification that changes nothing at runtime slips past it entirely -- there's no test failure to catch it, only inconsistent prose between a function's documented contract and an older caller written before that contract existed. **Repro:** none in the traditional Phase-R sense -- reordering the test doesn't change what it asserts. Fixed in `e25ebddc27`. |
| **New Angle I — Doc-contract-to-call-site parity (from the above two rows):** when a function's doc comment specifies a parameter's semantics/composition, or states a MUST-do-X-before-Y calling order, diff that stated contract against (a) every REAL call site's actual expression for that parameter, and (b) every EXISTING caller/test's actual call ORDER -- do this at doc-comment WRITE time (new function) and again any time a doc comment's contract is TIGHTENED or clarified in a later commit (existing function), not just when the function's own body changes. This is distinct from Angle A (is the code correct) and Angle H (is a spec requirement absent): the code can be 100% correct and the spec 100% satisfied while the DOC itself still under-specifies what it promises, or an older caller still contradicts a NEWER doc-stated rule that postdates it. Triggered by: (1) a NEW function introduced inside a FIX commit -- fix-commit review verifies "does this satisfy the reported finding," not "does this new function's doc fully specify its contract"; (2) a doc-only contract-tightening edit to an EXISTING function -- the repeat-offender re-derivation checks the function's own body against its restated contract, but never automatically sweeps existing callers/tests written before the tightening landed. Mechanical check: `grep` every doc comment for MUST/must-be/is-the (parameter semantics) and MUST...before/after/first (call ordering), then grep every call site of that function and manually confirm each satisfies both. |
| A repeat-offender function's fix closed the NAMED gap but the SAME derivation call re-derived a field the spec says must NOT be re-derived | `vlib/net/quic/key_update.v` `next_key_resolution`/`commit_new_update` | none yet — no existing angle asks "does this rotation correctly hold ONE field constant while others advance"; candidate for a future Angle A bullet | GitHub Copilot review on vlang/v#27881 (pullrequestreview-4885595852): both functions called `derive_packet_protection_keys(new_secret)`, which derives `key`/`iv`/`hp` all fresh — but RFC 9001 §6 rotates ONLY `key`/`iv` on a 1-RTT key update; `hp` is derived once per encryption level and never updates within it. Both call sites had already been touched once before on this branch (the round-5 Phase-R fix for the `note_successful_decrypt` stale-classification bug, same PR) — the repeat-offender full-contract-re-derivation rule (Phase 0 step 5) technically fired for `note_successful_decrypt` itself but never extended to its two sibling helper functions `next_key_resolution`/`commit_new_update`, which share the identical derive-then-store shape and the identical missed constraint. **Root cause, precisely named:** a prior Phase-R fix narrowly scoped to "re-derive the phase/generation classification correctly" verified against that ONE reported symptom; it never asked "are there OTHER fields this same derivation call touches that have their own, different rotation rule" — the RFC's per-field distinction (key/iv rotate, hp doesn't) was never enumerated against the function's own body as a checklist. Fixed by extracting a new `derive_updated_packet_protection_keys(next_secret, current_hp)` helper in `packet_protection.v` that derives fresh key/iv but returns the CALLER-supplied hp unchanged, used at both sites. **Repro:** `test_key_update_state_preserves_header_protection_key_across_update` asserts `resolution.keys.hp == original_hp`; fails on pre-fix code (hp differs after one update), passes after. |
| A fix's own doc comment claimed correctness "regardless of order" without checking a single-bit field's PERIOD against repeated events | `vlib/net/quic/key_update.v` `note_successful_decrypt` | none yet — candidate future check: "for any boolean/single-bit classification of a REPEATABLE event, does period-N aliasing after N repeats defeat the classification" | GitHub Copilot review on vlang/v#27881 (pullrequestreview-4885595852): the SAME function's doc comment (written in the PRIOR Phase-R fix, same PR) explicitly claimed "correct regardless of how many resolutions were computed before it, or in what order they are committed" — but that claim was only verified against a TWO-resolution, ONE-update scenario. After a SECOND key update, the phase bit (period-2) cycles back to its generation-0 value, so a genuinely old generation-0 packet's phase bit coincidentally matches the new generation-2 current phase — the exact "committed later, stale relative to now" hazard the doc comment claimed was solved, recurring one level up because the classification key (a 1-bit phase) has strictly less information than the thing it's meant to distinguish (an unbounded generation count). **Root cause, precisely named:** the prior fix's own regression test (`test_key_update_state_tolerates_resolving_before_committing`) only exercised ONE update — a test scoped to the reported symptom's exact scenario, not to the INVARIANT the fix claimed to establish ("correct regardless of order," which implicitly promises correctness across arbitrarily many updates, not just two-in-flight). This is `/codex-respond` Phase 4's "test-first for the invariant the fix establishes, not just the reported symptom" rule, missed on the fix's OWN prior application to this exact function. Fixed by replacing the phase-bit comparison with an absolute `current_generation`/`KeyResolution.generation` counter (unbounded, no periodicity) compared directly rather than re-derived from a lossy 1-bit projection. **Repro:** `test_key_update_state_second_update_does_not_corrupt_bookkeeping_via_stale_reordered_packet` reproduces a 2-update scenario; fails on pre-fix code (`min_pn_in_current_phase` corrupted from 200 to 50), passes after. |
| Missing pub fn doc comments (3x in one round) — `report-missing-fn-doc` CI check would have caught them, /vreview Angle A never checked for absence-of-comment as its own category | `vlib/net/quic/packet_number_space.v`/`key_update.v`/`handshake_confirm.v` | new Angle A bullet (mandatory doc-comment-presence check) | GitHub Copilot review on vlang/v#27881 (pullrequestreview-4885595852): `new_packet_number_spaces`, `KeyUpdateState.current_phase_bit`, `new_handshake_completion_state` were all pub fns with no doc comment. AGENTS.md's style rule only specifies the FORMAT once a comment exists ("Doc comments start with function name") — it was never operationalized as "every pub fn scan for absence." Added an explicit Angle A bullet requiring this as a pure grep, not a judgment call. **Repro:** none in the Phase-R sense (pure documentation, no runtime behavior); fixed by adding the 3 comments in `e2e09bcb59`. |
| Accumulating buffer's own doc comment claimed a cap "rarely binds in practice," but the buffer had no drain path at all, making the cap a hard total-size ceiling | `vlib/net/quic/stream_reassembly.v` `StreamReassembler.received`/`add` | new Angle D bullet ("accumulating-buffer capacity claims require a drain path") | GitHub Copilot review on vlang/v#27882 (pullrequestreview-4888843234): `max_stream_buffered_bytes`'s doc comment claimed real flow control would make the 1 MiB cap "rarely matter in practice," but `received` only ever appended (`<<`), never shrank — no `discard`/`consume`/drain existed. This made 1 MiB the EFFECTIVE TOTAL SIZE CEILING for any stream, even entirely in-order data, not a rarely-hit backstop on transient pending bytes. **Root cause:** I read this file in full during my own `/vreview` pass (confirmed by session transcript) and read the doc comment's claim, but never asked "does `received` ever get smaller" — a doc comment's stated safety rationale was taken at face value instead of traced against the field's actual lifecycle. Fixed by adding `base_offset` + a `discard(new_base)` method so a future caller (Phase 9's application-read path) can free consumed bytes, sliding the cap to bound buffered-but-unconsumed data instead of total stream size; also fixed `append_or_validate` to tolerate retransmissions of already-discarded data (silently, since there's nothing left to validate them against) and to check the cap right before mutation using the precise growth amount rather than an offset-based pre-check. **Repro:** `test_stream_reassembler_supports_streams_larger_than_the_buffering_limit` sends >1 MiB total via repeated add+discard; fails to reach that total on pre-fix code (rejected at the 1 MiB mark), passes after. |
| Self-inflicted: copied a parse-side overflow-safe check's SHAPE onto the encode side without re-verifying its precondition (bounded operands) held there | `vlib/net/quic/frame.v` `encode_stream_frame` | Angle G3 extended (encode-side/caller-supplied values are NOT automatically safe just because they mirror a parse-side check that is) | GitHub Copilot review on vlang/v#27882 (pullrequestreview-4888843234): in an EARLIER round of this same review, I added `if offset + u64(data.len) > max_varint` to `encode_stream_frame`, copied from `parse_crypto_frame`'s own (correctly overflow-safe) identical-looking check — but `parse_crypto_frame`'s safety argument (explicitly stated in ITS OWN doc comment) depends on both operands coming from `decode_varint`, which inherently bounds each to max_varint; `encode_stream_frame`'s `offset` is a caller-supplied parameter with NO such bound, so the sum itself could overflow u64 for an offset near `u64::MAX`, wrapping to a small value and silently passing the check it was meant to enforce. **Root cause:** copied a validated pattern's TEXT without re-deriving whether its PRECONDITION held at the new (encode, not parse) call site — the general lesson (sibling-parity copying needs precondition re-verification, not just pattern matching) generalizes Angle G3's existing "wire-derived" framing, which read as implicitly parse-side-only. Fixed with an overflow-safe form: bound `offset` alone first, then a subtraction-based comparison (`offset > max_varint - data.len`) that can never itself overflow. **Repro:** `test_encode_stream_frame_rejects_offset_near_u64_max_without_overflow` passes `offset = max_u64 - 3, data.len = 5`; silently succeeds (should reject) on pre-fix code, rejects with the correct 2^62 error after. |
| Fixed a bound-check gap on one struct's field but never grepped for the SAME field name on a sibling struct in the same file | `vlib/net/quic/frame.v` `StreamsBlockedFrame.maximum_streams` (vs. `MaxStreamsFrame.maximum_streams`, fixed one round earlier) | new Phase 0 step 7 addendum (grep the field/semantic name, not just the RFC section, after any bound fix) | GitHub Copilot review on vlang/v#27882 (pullrequestreview-4888843234): fixed `MaxStreamsFrame`'s missing RFC 9000 §4.6 2^60 cap in response to my own earlier `/vreview` finding, but never grepped `maximum_streams` across the rest of `frame.v` — `StreamsBlockedFrame` carries the identical semantic value with the identical requirement stated in ITS OWN RFC section (§19.14), a few dozen lines away in the same file. **Root cause:** anchored the sibling search on "which OTHER function covers the same RFC section I just cited" rather than "which OTHER struct/field shares this literal name" — the field-name grep is cheaper and would have found it immediately regardless of which RFC section documents each frame type's own copy of the requirement. Fixed by adding the identical bound to both `parse_streams_blocked_frame` and `encode_streams_blocked_frame`. **Repro:** `test_parse_max_streams_frame_rejects_value_above_2_pow_60`'s STREAMS_BLOCKED sibling test passes `max_initial_max_streams + 1`; silently accepted on pre-fix code, rejected after. |
| Reordered-arrival clobbered a terminal state (RFC 9000 §3.2 Reset Recvd) via an unrelated code path that never checked for it, 3x (same finding, 3 call sites) | `vlib/net/quic/stream.v` `StreamRecvHalf.note_size_known`/`note_data` (vs. `mark_reset_recvd`) | new Angle D bullet ("terminal state protection") | GitHub Copilot review on vlang/v#27882 (pullrequestreview-4888843234): `mark_reset_recvd` set `.reset_recvd`, but `note_size_known`/`note_data` — driven by ordinary STREAM-frame arrival, with no awareness that RESET_STREAM might have already been processed — unconditionally promoted state to `.data_recvd` once enough bytes had accumulated, with no check for the CURRENT state already being terminal. A STREAM frame that was in flight before a RESET_STREAM raced ahead of it (entirely normal packet reordering, not an attack) would silently un-reset the stream's classification. **Root cause:** no existing angle asked "for a terminal state value, does every OTHER writer of this field respect it" — the closest existing coverage (flag-contract/sibling-boolean-priority bullets) covers MULTIPLE flags interacting, not ONE terminal enum value against every other writer of the same field. Fixed by making both functions no-op once `state` is `.reset_recvd`/`.reset_read`. **Repro:** `test_recv_half_reset_recvd_is_not_clobbered_by_a_reordered_data_frame`/`..._size_known_frame` call `mark_reset_recvd` then `note_data`/`note_size_known`; state silently flips to `.data_recvd` on pre-fix code, stays `.reset_recvd` after. |

| New field-line branch (dynamic name reference, insertion unavailable) missing tracking its own sibling branches already had | `vlib/net/quic/qpack_encoder.v` `encode_field_section` | none new -- reinforces existing Phase 0 step 7 (sibling search), scoped WITHIN one function's own branches, not just across files | GPT-5.6 Luna review on PR vlang/v#28103 (2026-08-16, pasted directly by the user, no formal PR thread): the dynamic-name-reference literal branch (fires when a field's name matches an acknowledged dynamic entry but `can_insert` refuses insertion) never called `add_ref`, never appended to `referenced`, never bumped `required_insert_count` -- unlike the exact-match and post-insert branches in the SAME function, a few lines away, which already did all three. Produced a field section that could declare Required Insert Count 0 while still referencing the dynamic table, rejected by any correct decoder as QPACK_DECOMPRESSION_FAILED (RFC 9204 §2.2.3). **Root cause:** this same session's own `/vreview` pass on this exact diff checked two of the three sibling branches for the tracking obligation and stopped, exactly the "sibling parity within one function" gap Phase 0 step 7 already targets. Fixed: the third branch now add_refs, tracks, and bumps RIC identically to its siblings. **Repro:** `test_phase_r_dynamic_name_reference_without_insertion_tracks_required_insert_count`, Phase-R'd against unfixed code (reproduced the exact QPACK_DECOMPRESSION_FAILED the finding predicted) before the fix landed. |
| Truncated-vs-malformed conflation in a resumable decoder let malformed peer data hang forever instead of raising the required connection error | `vlib/net/quic/qpack_primitives.v`, `qpack_encoder_instructions.v`, `qpack_decoder_instructions.v` | none new -- the underlying question ("does a return-none-on-any-error resumable-parser wrapper verify its wrapped primitive has no distinct malformed-data failure mode") is semantic, not a syntactic grep | GPT-5.6 Luna review on PR vlang/v#28103 (2026-08-16): `decode_prefixed_int`/`decode_prefixed_string` had two genuinely distinct failure classes -- truncation (resumable, expected) and malformed data (an oversized integer past this implementation's own limit, invalid Huffman coding) -- both raised via a bare `error(...)`, indistinguishable to any caller. `decode_qpack_encoder_instruction`/`decode_qpack_decoder_instruction` then blanket-converted EVERY failure into `none` ("wait for more data") via their own Option-only return type, so malformed input from a broken/malicious peer looked identical to legitimately incomplete input -- the decoder would retry forever instead of raising QPACK_ENCODER_STREAM_ERROR and closing the connection (RFC 9204 §2.2.3, §3.2.2). **Root cause:** this project's own established "return none, not error, on truncation" pattern (Phase 10's `H3FrameDecoder`) is only safe when the wrapped primitive genuinely has no distinct malformed-data failure mode -- true for QUIC's own varint decoder (re-verified during this fix: `h3_stream_type.v`'s existing `or { return none }` remains correct, since `decode_varint` has no separate malformed class), false the moment an implementation adds its OWN additional limit on top of the wire format's own theoretical range, which is exactly what this project's `qpack_max_prefixed_int`/`qpack_max_string_literal_len` caps do. Fixed with a private sentinel error code distinguishing the two classes at the primitive level, and both instruction decoders redesigned from Option-returning to a result-struct + real `!` error propagation (V has no combined Result-Option type). Fixed identically in BOTH instruction decoders -- the decoder-stream sibling had the exact same bug shape with zero production callers yet, fixed anyway per the sibling-search discipline rather than stopping at the one instance reported. **Repro:** `test_phase_r_malformed_huffman_value_is_not_silently_treated_as_incomplete`, `test_decode_qpack_encoder_instruction_propagates_oversized_integer_error`, `test_phase_r_decoder_instruction_malformed_oversized_integer_propagates_error`, all Phase-R'd against unfixed code before the fix landed. |

| A capacity-reduction eviction path had no evictable-only guard, unlike its sibling insertion path | `vlib/net/quic/qpack_encoder.v` `QpackEncoder.set_capacity` (vs. `insert`/`can_insert`) | none new -- reinforces existing Phase 0 step 7 (sibling search: two functions of the same class -- here, "an operation that can evict dynamic-table entries" -- must share the same safety check) | GPT-5.6 Luna review, PR vlang/v#28103, round 2 (2026-08-18, pasted directly by the user): `set_capacity` called `dynamic_table.set_capacity()` unconditionally, which evicts oldest-first purely on `cur_size > capacity` with no reference/acknowledgment check at all -- unlike `insert()`, which goes through `can_insert()` first specifically to simulate eviction safety (RFC 9204 §2.1.1) before committing. Reducing capacity could silently evict an entry a not-yet-acknowledged field section still depended on, corrupting the encoder's own view of the table relative to what it had already told the peer. **Root cause:** this exact function existed unchanged since Phase 11's original commit and was never re-examined against `insert`'s sibling pattern in either the original implementation or the round-1 Luna fix's own sibling sweep (which was scoped to the round-1 bug's OWN reference-tracking shape, not to every other function touching the dynamic table). Fixed by adding `QpackDynamicTable.can_set_capacity`, mirroring `can_insert`'s simulate-eviction pattern, called from the encoder-level `set_capacity` before applying a reduction. **Repro:** `test_phase_r_set_capacity_rejects_reducing_below_a_referenced_entry`, Phase-R'd against unfixed code (reproduced the predicted silent eviction via `dynamic_table.get(0)` erroring "evicted or nonexistent") before the fix landed. |
| Sibling encode/decode functions diverged on an implementation-limit guard; only one side had it | `vlib/net/quic/qpack_field_line.v` `encode_ric` (vs. `decode_ric`) | none new -- reinforces Phase 0 step 7's sibling-parity diff, applied to an encode/decode pair rather than two branches of one function | GPT-5.6 Luna review, PR vlang/v#28103, round 2 (2026-08-18): `encode_ric` computed `full_range := 2 * qpack_max_entries(max_table_capacity)` and divided by it (`req_insert_count % full_range`) with no zero check; `decode_ric`, two lines below in the same file, already had `if full_range == 0 { return error(...) }` for the identical computation. `qpack_max_entries` returns 0 whenever `max_table_capacity < 32` (Luna's report cited `< 64`, off by the factor-of-2 in `full_range`'s own definition -- confirmed and corrected during triage), a realistic input since `SETTINGS_QPACK_MAX_TABLE_CAPACITY` is peer-controlled. Crashed the whole process on modulo-by-zero rather than returning a graceful error. **Root cause:** the RFC 9204 §4.5.1.1 pseudocode both functions transcribe never states this bound explicitly (it documents the modular-arithmetic FORMULA, not the degenerate-input guard), so a "verify against RFC pseudocode" reading reproduces the RFC's own implicit-range assumption instead of surfacing the gap -- only a direct encode-vs-decode sibling diff (not a spec-conformance reading) would have caught it, and neither the original implementation nor the round-1 fix's sibling sweep (scoped to a different bug class) performed one for this specific pair. Fixed identically to `decode_ric`'s guard, rippling `encode_ric`/`encode_field_section_prefix`/`encode_field_section` to Result-returning signatures. **Repro:** `test_phase_r_encode_ric_rejects_capacity_too_small_instead_of_dividing_by_zero`, Phase-R'd against unfixed code (reproduced the exact "modulo by zero" panic) before the fix landed. |
| New function's own tail case was wrong relative to the sibling it was built to mirror -- caught by the review's own mandatory contract check, not an external reviewer | `vlib/net/quic/qpack_dynamic_table.v` `can_set_capacity` (vs. `can_insert`) | none new -- this IS the existing Phase 0 step 6/7 check working as intended; logged as a positive data point, not a gap | Self-caught during this session's own `/vreview` pass on the P1 fix above (2026-08-19), via the mandatory fresh-contract-from-scratch + sibling-parity-diff check for any NEW function: `can_set_capacity`'s eviction-simulation loop returned bare `true` whenever it exited normally, instead of `can_insert`'s own tail (`return freed >= needed`) -- wrong for any negative `new_capacity` (the loop's `over`/`freed` bookkeeping can never `return false` via the entries-exhausted path, since nothing evictable existed to trigger the per-entry guard, yet freed never actually reached what was needed). Unreachable via the only current caller (`QpackEncoder.set_capacity`, which only ever passes an already-validated non-negative value), but `can_set_capacity` is `pub` and its correctness was written as if that caller-side guarantee didn't exist. **Why this is logged despite not being a miss:** it demonstrates the sibling-parity-diff step catching a real defect in freshly-written code within the SAME turn it was written, before any external reviewer saw it -- the corpus mechanism worked exactly as designed for once, worth a row precisely because most rows here are the opposite case. **Repro:** `test_dynamic_table_can_set_capacity_rejects_negative_capacity_even_when_table_empty`, Phase-R'd against the just-written (not yet committed) buggy code before the fix landed. |

| A THIRD sibling map right next to two the Phase A adversarial pass already fixed for the identical unbounded-growth bug class went unfixed, despite an inline comment rationalizing it | `vlib/net/quic/h3_conn.v` `H3Conn.dead_request_streams` (vs. `request_streams`/`request_decoders`, fixed in the same `fail_request_stream`) | `detect_unbounded_accum.sh` generalized (was hardcoded to receiver `c.` and only matched `<<`/`++`/`+=`, never a map-key assignment; H3Conn's receiver is `h` and the growth is `h.dead_request_streams[stream_id] = true` -- the detector matched NOTHING in this file, silently) + `run_detectors.sh` manifest widened from `^h2_.*\.v` to `.*\.v` (the detector never ran against ANY quic/h3 file at all, independent of the two script bugs -- the more fundamental root cause) | Maintainer JalonSolov's "Local AI Findings" review comment on PR vlang/v#28129 (2026-08-21, commit `96ea26f1e4`): `fail_request_stream` prunes `request_streams`/`request_decoders` on both the failure and success paths (that pruning was itself one of the Phase A adversarial pass's own 9 confirmed fixes on this same PR) but leaves `dead_request_streams` growing forever -- a doc comment right at the site explicitly rationalized this ("deliberately left alone: it is a tiny, permanent bool marker"), unlike the two just-fixed siblings which had no such rationalization before their fix. **Root cause, precisely named -- two independent, stacked layers:** (1) mechanical: `detect_unbounded_accum.sh` was written against H2's `mut c H2Conn` convention and only recognized `<<`/`++`/`+=` growth; it never saw h3_conn.v at all (`run_detectors.sh`'s manifest scoped it to `^h2_.*\.v` filenames only) and even if it had, its receiver-name hardcoding and missing map-assignment pattern meant it would still have matched zero fields in this file -- confirmed by running it directly pre-fix and observing a false-clean pass with 0 fields found. (2) semantic (the multi-agent Phase A adversarial-verification pass, independent of the detector): the pass found and fixed the IDENTICAL bug class twice in the SAME function, on the two fields sitting immediately next to this one, but stopped short of the third -- most plausibly because `dead_request_streams` had its own confident, already-written justification in the code, which reads as "already considered, not a bug" and invites trust rather than the same scrutiny a skeptic lens would apply to an unjustified field. This is the `[[feedback_vreview_repeat_offender_gate]]` sibling-search gap recurring in a different reviewer (a Workflow of LLM lenses, not Codex) -- fixing A and B in one function does not reliably re-trigger scrutiny of C sitting right next to them, especially when C carries its own rationalizing comment. Fixed by adding `H3Conn.prune_terminal_dead_streams()`, called once per `drive()` cycle after `drain_known_peer_streams`, which deletes a `dead_request_streams` entry once `QuicConn.stream_recv_status` reports the underlying stream's receive side has itself reached a terminal state (reset or fin received) -- at that point RFC 9000 guarantees no further frames for that stream ID can ever arrive, so the bookkeeping is no longer needed; entries for streams the peer hasn't finished draining yet are deliberately left in place (fail_request_stream's own doc comment explains why: the peer may still legally keep sending, and this module has no STOP_SENDING API yet to tell it to stop). **Repro:** `test_h3_conn_prunes_dead_request_stream_once_peer_side_fully_terminal` in `h3_conn_test.v`, Phase-R'd against the unfixed code (asserted `dead_request_streams` still held `{0: true}` after the peer finished the stream -- the exact unbounded-growth symptom) before the fix landed. **Detector spot-check after widening the manifest to `.*\.v`:** running it against `h2_conn.v` (a previously-covered file) still produces the same 3 fields it always did (`next_stream_id`/`pending`/`rbuf`), confirming no regression in existing coverage; running it against the now-newly-scanned `h3_conn.v` also surfaces `blocked_sections`/`ignored_peer_streams`/`peer_qpack_encoder_buf`/`peer_qpack_decoder_buf` as candidates -- all four manually verified as false positives (respectively: pruned via array-filter-rebuild, an idiom the `.delete()`-based CAPPED check doesn't recognize; transitively bounded by this connection's own `local_max_streams_uni`, which this implementation's current design never renews via a follow-up MAX_STREAMS frame -- see `conn.v:206-212`'s own documented follow-up; and bounded by `max_h3_control_plane_buffered_bytes` enforced in the sibling file `h3_qpack_streams.v`, outside this detector's single-file scope, twice). None required a code fix; logged here so a future session doesn't re-spend the same verification effort. **Known remaining detector gaps, left as follow-ups rather than fixed now:** array-filter-rebuild pruning (`x = kept`) isn't recognized as a bound (would clear the `blocked_sections`/`rbuf`-style false positives); map-VALUE increment (`m[k] += 1`/`m[k]++`) isn't matched as a growth pattern at all; cross-file bound enforcement (the QPACK buffer case) is invisible to a single-file detector by design. **Still owed:** `code-review-reference.html` sync (Windows-only path, `%USERPROFILE%\Documents\code-review-reference.html`, unreachable from this Mac session -- do it from the Windows machine next). |

| Verified the wrong shell-quoting property (word-splitting) instead of the one the reviewer actually flagged (parameter/command substitution) | `vlib/v3/tests/heap_attr_struct_alloc_test.v` `build_v3_for_heap_attr` | none new -- candidate future check: "for any hand-wrapped path interpolated into `os.execute()`, require `os.quoted_path()` rather than literal quote characters -- a wrapper must defend against word-splitting AND `$`/`` ` ``/`$()` expansion, not just one" | Codex round 2 on PR vlang/v#28185 (pullrequestreview-5044197146, self-inflicted -- this is my own prior-round fix, not a pre-existing bug): round 1 (pullrequestreview-5033468734) fixed word-splitting on every `os.execute()` path argument in this file except the compound `-path` value, which already had literal double quotes (`-path "${vlib_dir}|@vlib|@vmodules"`) -- verified "sufficient" via an argv-boundary probe that only confirmed the shell still saw it as one argument. Codex named the actual gap: POSIX double quotes block word-splitting/globbing but NOT `$VAR`/`` `cmd` ``/`$(cmd)` expansion, so a checkout path containing a literal `$` would have the shell substitute it before v3 ever saw the flag. **Root cause, precisely named:** "verify empirically, not by argument" (`/codex-respond` Phase 2) was followed correctly in MECHANISM but not in SCOPE -- I ran a real probe and it genuinely passed, but the probe established one property (argv-boundary integrity) while I treated the green result as evidence for a broader, unstated claim ("no shell metacharacter can corrupt this value") that probe never tested. Shell quoting has (at least) three independent properties -- word-splitting, glob expansion, parameter/command substitution -- and picking one to test without enumerating all three first is indistinguishable, from the outside, from having verified the right one. Fixed by wrapping the whole compound value with `os.quoted_path()` (single quotes on Unix, which block all three) instead of hand-written double quotes. Phase-R'd before fixing: `os.execute('echo "${evil}|@vlib|@vmodules"')` with `evil = '/tmp/repo$HOME'` printed the shell-expanded home directory on the buggy double-quote pattern; the identical value through `os.execute('echo ${os.quoted_path(...)}')` round-tripped the literal string unchanged. **Repro:** the two-line standalone script above -- exit 0 both times, but only the `os.quoted_path` line's output matches the input literally. |

Not a net.http/quic row and not an external-review miss: a self-directed
investigation ("dig into why v3 exceeds 10GB RSS", 2026-08-28) run through
`/phase-r` on the V3 compiler itself. Logged here because phase-r step 8 owns the
write-up regardless of how the finding originated; the reusable content is the
two ways the prior session's measurement pointed the fix at the wrong target.

| "Slowest `[ttime]` sub-phase" and "coarse RSS bracket" each aimed the fix at the wrong code; the real 9GB was two transient-allocation bugs in V3's native-input path, both amplified by `-prealloc`/`-gc none` | `vlib/v3/driver/driver.v` `c_typedef_is_function_pointer` (dominant, ~8GB) + `vlib/v3/gen/c/cleanc.v` `cache_native_inputs_language` (~2.2GB) | grep guard candidate: `\[[^]]*\.\.\][ ]*\.index\(` / `source\[.*\.\.\]` inside a scan loop flags the "re-slice the tail every iteration" antipattern; not wired as a detector | Self-found while chasing intermittent `v3 compiler memory usage reached ~10285 MiB RSS` building `vguard` (`~/repo/vguard`, a GUI app pulling OpenSSL via `crypto.ecdsa`, Cocoa/AppKit via `vglyph` accessibility, and sokol/fontstash/stb/mbedtls single-header C libs at once), which forced silent fallback to the V1 stable compiler. Base commit `40c4b0e5` on `master`. **Reproduction = direct measurement, not a test:** `V3CACHE=<fresh-dir> v -v -no-memory-limit -o out .` on a COLD cache (the failure is cold-only; warm builds peak ~750MB) -> `check` stage peak **9058 MB**. `runtime.used_memory()` RSS-bracket probes at every sub-phase boundary of the `check` stage plus inside `prepare_v3_cache_external_inputs`, plus a per-header read counter (`V3_DIAG_SCAN_READS`) and a compile-time switch to disable all V3 prealloc scopes (`V3_DIAG_NO_PREALLOC_SCOPES`). **Prior session's conclusion -- REFUTED:** it said "the multi-GB cost is `prealloc_scope_begin()`'s own upfront arena reservation." The allocator opens a scoped arena with a single **256 KB** block (`prealloc_scope_block_size`, `vlib/builtin/prealloc.c.v:36`) -- not GBs. Disabling every V3 prealloc scope made the peak **worse** (9058 -> 10955 MB, 12.2 GB physical footprint): V3 `-prealloc` builds run `-gc none`, so with no scope to bulk-free at exit, nothing is reclaimed at all. The scope was *mitigating* the spike, not causing it. **Actual root cause #1 (dominant, ~8GB):** `c_typedef_is_function_pointer(source, name)` did `source[offset..].index(name)` once per loop iteration -- V `string[a..]` allocates a fresh copy of the whole remaining tail of `source`. Called once per typedef name per native header from `register_native_source_typedefs` (inside a retained `-prealloc` stage scope): sokol_gfx.h / stb_truetype.h / mbedtls / openssl headers x hundreds of typedef names x per-iteration tail copies = multi-GB of retained scratch. Fix: `source.index_after(name, offset)` (scans in place, no allocation). **Root cause #2 (~2.2GB):** `cache_native_inputs_language` recursively inlined each header's ENTIRE transitive `#include` closure into a `strings.Builder` -- once per `#include` site, fresh `seen` map each call (no cross-site dedup), no size cap -- purely to compute one bool ("needs Objective-C?"). Fix: memoize the bool per include argument, early-exit the loop once `need_objc` is set, and wrap each still-needed inline in a disposable `cgen_worker_scope_*` so its closure is freed as soon as the bool is read. (A third change re-based from the prior session: `c_collect_external_input_tree` collects `[]string` line refs and does one byte-copy after recursion instead of a second full-size builder held across the descent -- real but small, ~200MB.) **Result:** cold `check` stage peak **9058 -> 974 MB**; whole cold build now peaks at `cgen` ~**1975 MB**, well under the 10 GiB cap -- the intermittent V1 fallback is resolved. `vlib/v3/gen/c/target_test.v` 1/1, `vlib/v3/modulecache/` 2/2, output binary byte-identical size. **Falsified hypotheses, recorded so they are not re-tread:** (a) prealloc scope-open reservation (this session -- refuted as above); (b) `cache_native_inputs_language` being the whole story (it was ~2.2 of ~9 GB); (c) unfiltered `-I` dirs (those came from V1's link-time cflags dump, not V3's scanner); (d) `c_record_include_macro_definition` retaining every `#define` value (it keeps only literal `<...>`/quoted values, else a bool); (e) repeated real `cc -E` of `gg_darwin.m` (`V3_CACHE_TRACE=1`: fired once, failed immediately). **Methodology lessons:** (1) the slowest `[ttime]` sub-phase is not necessarily the memory-peak sub-phase -- the prior session pinned 8.9GB on `ck native inputs` because it was 70% of wall time; after making it 4x faster the `check` peak was unchanged. Measure RSS *at* the peak. (2) a point-probe (`used_memory()` at call boundaries) misses a transient that allocates-and-frees between probes -- the real spike lived inside a scope-wrapped call whose "after" probe read low because the scope had already bulk-freed; probe *inside* the suspicious call or bracket at finer grain. (3) sanity-check magnitude against input volume before coding a fix (334 headers / 8.8 MB cannot triple-copy to GBs -- but they can if an O(n^2) per-name tail-copy sits in the loop). **Tree state:** **MERGED as PR vlang/v#28237** (squash commit `d06da0a960` on `master`, 2026-08-28); local `master` fast-forwarded, branch deleted local+fork. Was 2 commits: the three fixes + `test_cache_native_inputs_language_header_branch_and_memo_key`. `/adversarial-review` (19 agents, 4 lenses) run on `dfd33a3a32` -- the index_after equivalence, prealloc-scope-lifetime, and memo-key-soundness lenses all returned empty; the one confirmed finding was a pure test-coverage gap (the modified `cache_native_inputs_language` header branch had no test -- the only caller-test used an `.mm` source that takes a different branch), closed by `24330b7474`'s `test_cache_native_inputs_language_header_branch_and_memo_key`, which was Phase-R'd (fails if the quoted-include memo key drops its including-file component). **Still owed:** `code-review-reference.html` sync (Windows-only path, unreachable from this Mac -- do it from the Windows machine next). |

| Third round of the same file patching a THIRD shell-quoting property instead of eliminating the shell dependency itself | `vlib/v3/tests/heap_attr_struct_alloc_test.v` `build_v3_for_heap_attr`/`heap_attr_run_good`/`heap_attr_generated_c` (vs. sibling `secure_command_test.v` `build_secure_command_v3` and its call sites) | new Phase 0 step 7 corollary: after the SAME file/function absorbs 2+ narrow fixes for the SAME bug class, the next fix should ask "does an existing sibling eliminate this whole class" before patching a third instance of it | Codex round 3 on PR vlang/v#28185 (pullrequestreview-5077561236): round 1 fixed word-splitting, round 2 fixed `$`/backtick/`$()` substitution -- both in the same `os.execute()`-based helpers. Round 3 found a third property in the same family: on Windows, `os.execute()` delegates to `cmd.exe`, and `os.quoted_path()`'s Windows branch only adds double quotes, which don't block `%VAR%` expansion. **Root cause, precisely named:** each of the first two rounds treated the finding as "one more thing this string needs escaped," never asking whether `os.execute()` + hand-built command strings was the wrong TOOL for this job -- despite `vlib/v3/tests/secure_command_test.v`, sitting in the SAME directory, already establishing the correct fix (`v3.cmdexec.run()`, an argv-based `os.new_process()` wrapper that never invokes a shell on any platform, so none of the three quoting properties can even apply) and already having a dedicated adversarial test (`test_source_directives_cannot_inject_shell_commands`) proving it. This is Phase 0 step 7's sibling-parity check, but the miss compounds ACROSS rounds: round 1 should have found `secure_command_test.v` via "does an existing test in this exact directory invoke the v3 compiler safely" before ever writing `os.execute()` in a brand-new test file, and rounds 2 and 3 each had a second and third opportunity to notice the SAME file kept re-absorbing point-fixes for the SAME bug class and ask why, instead of accepting the frame the previous round's fix set (isolated word-splitting fix → isolated substitution fix → isolated %VAR% fix, three separate patches instead of one class-eliminating rewrite). Fixed by migrating all three functions to `cmdexec.run()`, verified via a design-level proof (`CreateProcessW` called directly, `vlib/os/process_windows.c.v:201`, no `cmd.exe` in the path) rather than a literal repro, since this Mac session has no Windows environment -- stated as an explicit limitation rather than glossed over. **Repro:** none in the traditional sense for the Windows-specific failure (no Windows access); the fix's soundness rests on the cited source proof plus `secure_command_test.v`'s own pre-existing, re-verified-green coverage of the identical pattern. |
| Two caller-supplied output destinations checked independently ("must not already exist") but never against EACH OTHER: the observation path could alias an evidence file that the same call writes first, so the later write silently replaced the evidence and the call still reported success | `thirdparty/tccbin_automation/bin/toolchain_observation.v` `collect_toolchain_observation`, vlang/v#31426 at `a22cb8b24b` (found by medvednikov, issuecomment-6532336165) | Regression tests in `tests/toolchain_observation_test.v`: an observation path inside the evidence dir (direct, symlinked, differently cased on macOS) is refused before probing, and on macOS arm64 the reviewer's exact `<fresh-dir>/<evidence-sha256>` case. Fix compares directory identity (dev+inode), not path spelling, and re-hashes every evidence file after all writes | **Existing rule that should have caught it:** none that named it. Angle A/D cover existence checks and partial writes, and the adversarial security finder raised "destination writes are not fail-closed" (TOCTOU, parent symlinks), which was refuted 3-0, but no rule asks whether two outputs of ONE call can name the same file. **Why it failed:** each destination was audited in isolation; the evidence file name is derived (its own SHA-256), so the alias is invisible until you ask which names the call itself will create. **New enforcement:** checklist rule: for any function writing 2+ caller-located outputs, enumerate every path it will create (including derived names) and require an explicit pairwise no-alias check, compared by identity, not spelling (case-insensitive APFS, symlinks). Repro: the new tests FAIL on `a22cb8b24b` (macOS case: collection succeeds, message ''), PASS after the fix; disabling the identity check makes the post-write re-hash fire instead. |
| Unbounded-accum, PARTIAL cap: `H3FrameDecoder.pending` capped only for DATA/HEADERS; unknown/grease and the other defined frame types buffered their full declared payload on a request stream | `vlib/net/quic/h3_frame.v` / `h3_conn.v` `dispatch_request_stream_frames`, vlang/v#28164 at `29a933e6c3`, Codex discussion_r3957392454 | `detect_unbounded_accum.sh` DID flag `pending: NO CAP FOUND`; the flag was dismissed because a cap existed for SOME frame types. Rule: a cap keyed on a peer-chosen discriminator must be checked for EVERY discriminator value | Present-but-dismissed; repro loops over every frame type |
| Unbounded-accum, rationale-overridden: `H3Server.peer_by_str` stored every UDP source address before validation, never pruned, with an in-code "Deliberately unbounded for v1" comment | `vlib/net/http/h3_server_d_http3.v` `serve`, vlang/v#28164 at `29a933e6c3`, Codex discussion_r3957392459 | `detect_unbounded_accum.sh` DID flag `peer_by_str: NO CAP FOUND`; the in-code justification was accepted. Rule: an "intentionally unbounded" comment on a map keyed by PRE-AUTHENTICATION input is a finding, not a waiver | Present-but-overridden; repro sends junk from 16 source ports |
| Missing RFC MUST: client silently ignored a server-initiated bidirectional stream instead of H3_STREAM_CREATION_ERROR (RFC 9114 §6.1) | `vlib/net/quic/h3_conn.v` `handle_quic_events`, vlang/v#28164 at `29a933e6c3`, Codex discussion_r3957392463 | No conformance-matrix row for §6.1's client-side clause (only §6.2.x stream-type rows) | Missing; added matrix row |
| Test-only diff certifying an "already fixed upstream" panic ran in Lite mode; other writers of the same counter still broke the slice: `.filter(...)` folds panicked `slice bounds out of range (8 >= 6)`, and `$d`/`${}`/`if` targets let `source_token_count` fall behind and lose the target type | `vlib/v/gen/fastc/expr.v` `validate_expression_stream_token` (`expression_tokens[..source_token_count]`) and the writers in `read_expression_with_prefix_mode_impl`; vlang/v#31797 at `ec1d326b37`, found by `/adversarial-review` round 1 (correctness lens), 2026-11-03 | Existing rule: Angle A "thread a cross-cutting requirement through EVERY producer" + Phase 0 step 7 sibling parity. Why it failed: mode selection picked Lite because only `_test.v` changed, so `expr.v` was never read in full; "fixed" rested on one reproduced shape; Angle J mutated only the reader (`target_type := u32(0)`); the sibling table compared harnesses, not fix sites. New mechanism: vreview rev 75, Lite exception (a test certifying a fix takes the mode of the code under test and lists every writer of the corrected state) + Angle J "mutate the writers, not only the reader" | Present-but-switched-off by mode selection; repro: `counts[bag.items.filter(it > 1).len] = 7` through `index_or_assignment_target_test.v`'s harness at `ec1d326b37` panics; passes at `e8eaeed79a` |
| Test discrimination rules applied loosely: four test gaps after two clean `/vreview` passes -- (1) the `function_c_names[key] or { fastc_signature_c_name(...) }` fallback (path of the real `builtin` collision) reached in no mode, the test collided only under target libc headers where the table is always filled; (2) no main-module (undotted key) case; (3) "method keeps its name" logged as an unpinnable GAP, a method-renaming swap mutant passed; (4) compact-mode coverage rested on the incidental `starts_with('math')` naming rule | `vlib/v/gen/fastc/fastc.v` `generate_source_pieces` rename block + `self_build_regressions_test.v`; vlang/v#31799 at `50f2f0e106`, found by `/adversarial-review` rounds 1-2 (tests lens), 2026-11-03 | Existing rule: Angle J "each branch of each changed lookup", "every clause of a compound condition", "nearest wrong implementation". Why it failed: one table row per condition rather than per branch and mode; while writing the test the author saw compact mode pass without the fix and did not record that mode as non-covering; GAP accepted after considering runtime output only, not the generated C; only self-chosen mutants tried; a "no mutation tried" row and an untargeted suite ("all 223 tests") counted as evidence. New enforcement (rev 76): Angle J bullets "one row per branch, with the configuration that reaches it", "a GAP must name every observable", "assert that the incidental path was taken", "no row counts without a targeted test and a tried mutation"; table gains mode and observables columns. No detector: `or {}` branches of new code need the diff, which detectors do not read | Present-but-applied-loosely; repro: at `50f2f0e106` the test passes under each of: fallback replaced by `or { continue }`, rename gated on `prefs.target_libc_headers`, method rename instead of free fn; at `73ec1c0b6f` all fail |
| Rename reused a name a sibling had allocated: the FastC free-function rename always chose `__vf_function_<name>`, already taken by the constant collision pass, giving conflicting C types on a program that built on the base | `vlib/v/gen/fastc/fastc.v` `generate_source_pieces`; vlang/v#31799 at `73ec1c0b6f`, medvednikov review 5759552885, comment 4460505912, 2026-11-03 | Existing rule: Phase 0 step 7 sibling parity. Why it failed: compared for shape only, not for which names the sibling had taken. Rule: siblings allocating from one namespace are collision sources; one parity row per allocator | Present, scope too narrow; fixed in `63b845f6ad`, repro failed first; SKILL.md bullet (rev 77) |
| Assertion true only on the host target: `self_build_regressions_test.v` asserted `i64` for V's `int`, false on linux/x86 | `vlib/v/gen/fastc/self_build_regressions_test.v`; vlang/v#31799 at `73ec1c0b6f`, medvednikov review 5759552885, comment 4460505921, 2026-11-03 | No rule. Rule: a literal in an assertion must hold on every target the test's guard admits; read it from the code that decides it | Missing; fixed in `63b845f6ad`, repro failed first; Angle J bullet (rev 77) |

## Sections folded in from the Windows memory copy

These write-ups were logged only in the Windows machine's project-memory copy of this
corpus and were folded in on 2026-11-03, after the move above. Wiki-style `[[...]]` links name
Claude project-memory notes, which are not part of this repo.

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang-default-ca-store`, PR vlang/v#28406 (default CA trust store +
`validate` default flipped to `true`, plus 5 new QUIC frame parsers found needed
while verifying HTTP/3 against real servers). External review ran as ~14 separate
rounds over 2026-09-06/07 — first two rounds relayed by JalonSolov ("Local AI"
findings) and answered via this project's own `/codex-respond`-shaped loop; the
remaining dozen ran as `medvednikov` (the actual maintainer) driving `@codex
review` directly against the pushed branch, entirely outside this session's own
`/vreview` invocations. **Root-cause context, not an excuse:** mid-investigation
of this same PR, the `/vreview` skill itself was discovered EMPTY on this machine
(both `~/.claude/skills/vreview` and the repo-local copy had zero files, and
"vreview" had silently dropped out of the available-skills list) — see
[[vreview-skill-data-loss-2026-09-07]]. Whether it was already empty during the
ORIGINAL implementation work on this PR, or broke sometime after, is unconfirmed
— but it means the volume of findings below cannot be fully attributed to gaps in
the skill's CONTENT; a broken/unavailable tool is at least as plausible a cause,
and is exactly why a broken skill is now this corpus's own top-priority class of
miss (see the row below and [[vreview-skill-data-loss-2026-09-07]]).

| Class | File:line | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| New wire-format frame parser accepted a value RFC 9000 explicitly forbids, WITH a regression test locking in the wrong behavior | `vlib/net/quic/frame.v` `parse_new_token_frame` | new Angle E callout (brand-new frame types are, by definition, uncovered by the matrix) | `parse_new_token_frame` treated `Token Length == 0` as legal; RFC 9000 §19.7: "the Token field MUST NOT be empty ... a client MUST treat receipt of a NEW_TOKEN frame with an empty Token field as a connection error of type FRAME_ENCODING_ERROR." A same-session regression test, `test_new_token_frame_accepts_zero_length_token`, asserted the WRONG behavior was correct — worse than no test, since a future review sees green and assumes coverage. **Why local review missed it:** the frame was new in this PR; `quic_conformance_matrix.md` never got a row for it before shipping, so Angle E's mandatory "new area → add the matrix section" instruction was never triggered. Fixed by JalonSolov/medvednikov round: reject `length == 0` with `FRAME_ENCODING_ERROR`, invert the regression test. |
| Every new frame parser this PR added was missing truncation/bounds checks on at least one fixed-length field | `vlib/net/quic/frame.v` `parse_new_connection_id_frame`/`parse_retire_connection_id_frame`/`parse_path_challenge_or_response_frame` | new Angle E callout (enumerate every RFC field, two passes: length + value-constraint) | Across ~6 external-review rounds, truncated/missing Sequence Number, Retire Prior To, Length, connection-ID, and stateless-reset-token fields on NEW_CONNECTION_ID; truncated Sequence Number on RETIRE_CONNECTION_ID; truncated NEW_TOKEN Token Length — none returned `FRAME_ENCODING_ERROR` (0x07) on a short buffer, each found in a SEPARATE round rather than all at once. **Why local review missed it:** no single check asked "for this new frame, list every fixed-length field the RFC defines, confirm each one is bounds-checked before being read" — happy-path round-trip tests existed for all five parsers and passed the whole time. Fixed across commits `4f6987481c`, `40de5c1eba`, `ea95c51cb7`, `59402bd55c`. |
| "Out of scope: migration" scope note used to justify skipping RFC requirements independent of migration | `vlib/net/quic/conn.v` `dispatch_one_rtt_frame`'s generic `else` arm | new Angle H bullet (scope notes must enumerate what they exempt) | NEW_CONNECTION_ID/RETIRE_CONNECTION_ID were dispatched to a no-op `else` with a comment that connection migration is out of v1 scope. True for USING an alternate CID; false for RFC 9000 §5.1.2 (stop using retired CIDs) and §19.16 (PROTOCOL_VIOLATION on an unissued sequence number) — both fully actionable against this endpoint's single locally-issued CID (sequence 0) regardless of migration. Also missing: §5.1.1 stateless-reset-token uniqueness across peer CID sequence numbers, active_connection_id_limit enforcement (including registering `preferred_address`'s CID as sequence 1), and CID-uniqueness/duplicate-consistency checks — all found and fixed piecemeal across commits `4f6987481c`, `ea95c51cb7`, `57270cef3f`, `4486f6ba49`, `e53eb60fcc`. **Why local review missed it:** the scope note read as broader than it was, and nothing forced a line-by-line check of which RFC §5.1.x/§19.15-16 clauses were actually migration-dependent vs. independent. |
| Sibling TLS BACKEND (not sibling function) had the exact same insecure-default bug this PR was written to fix | `vlib/net/openssl/*` (OpenSSL backend) vs. `vlib/net/mbedtls/*` (this PR's own headline fix) | new Phase 0 step 7 bullet (sibling can be a build-flag-selected backend) | This PR's OWN premise was fixing `net.mbedtls`'s `validate: false`-defaulting to `MBEDTLS_SSL_VERIFY_OPTIONAL` (verifies but never aborts the handshake). `net.openssl` — a second TLS backend selectable in the SAME codebase, touched by nobody's sibling-search step — never called `SSL_VERIFY_PEER` or checked the requested hostname/IP AT ALL, in either its default or explicit-`verify` path. A third backend (SChannel) turned out to already be correct (OS trust store) but undocumented. **Why local review missed it:** Phase 0 step 7's sibling search existed but had no bullet naming "a different backend behind the same build flag" as a sibling category — every existing example was same-file or same-layer. Fixed in `e64eb76ad6` (SSL_VERIFY_PEER + hostname/IP verification + a positive/negative OpenSSL regression) and `aef1f1376c` (SChannel doc). |
| A newly-introduced "portable, non-retryable" error code had to be re-applied to each of 4+ independent failure producers, across many separate rounds | `vlib/net/http/response.v` (retry gate) vs. `net.mbedtls`/`net.openssl`/SChannel/HTTP-3-async/pooled/waiter/proxy error-delivery paths | extends existing Angle A bullet ("error code dropped across a boundary") to "error code must reach every producer of the class" | Once a portable certificate-validation error code existed so HTTP retry logic could recognize "don't retry this," it had to be independently threaded through: mbedTLS CA-bundle/cert/key parse failures (including a nonzero-but-"partial success" mbedTLS return code that was being silently treated as success), OpenSSL default/custom trust-store setup failures, OpenSSL client-cert/key and SNI setup failures, HTTP/3's async certificate-failure delivery, and HTTP CONNECT/SOCKS5 proxy TLS setup failures — each was its OWN external-review round (`ea95c51cb7`, `cdd947e9d1`, `1feea49042`, `dbef24ae11`), not one sweep. **Why local review missed it:** no check asked "grep every OTHER producer of this same logical failure class" once the first classified-error fix landed — the existing struct-field-boundary bullet was scoped to ONE field, not "this failure class, codebase-wide." |
| Resource leak on error paths in files the diff's own default-value change never touched | `vlib/net/http/` HTTP CONNECT + SOCKS5 TLS setup; `net.mbedtls` shutdown | reinforces existing Angle H free-path bullet, via the NEW "every other consumer of a changed default" sibling-search addition | Failed HTTP CONNECT/SOCKS5 TLS setup left partial SSL state unfreed and the TCP socket open; mbedTLS shutdown wasn't idempotent and didn't safely free pre-handshake state. Neither file was directly edited by the CA-bundle/`validate`-default change, but flipping `validate`'s default to `true` makes untrusted-proxy TLS setup fail MORE often, exercising an error path that was already broken and simply hadn't been reached before. **Why local review missed it:** Angle H's free-path check ran against files the diff touched; it never extended to files that only became MORE reachable because of a changed default elsewhere. Fixed in `40de5c1eba` (proxy cleanup + mbedTLS shutdown idempotency, with a regression test). |
| Deep QUIC connection-ID protocol-state requirements kept surfacing one at a time across many rounds | `vlib/net/quic/conn.v` (`PeerConnectionIdSet`/`LocalConnectionIdSet` machinery, added post-hoc) | `quic_conformance_matrix.md` §5.1.1/§5.1.2/§19.15/§19.16 rows (now populated) | Beyond the two rows above: NEW_CONNECTION_ID duplicate sequence/content consistency, CID uniqueness, `CONNECTION_ID_LIMIT_ERROR` on exceeding `active_connection_id_limit`, `preferred_address`'s CID registered as sequence 1 counting toward that limit, PROTOCOL_VIOLATION when a peer selects a zero-length initial CID. All real, all eventually fixed (`ea95c51cb7` through `e53eb60fcc`), but each was its own round rather than one requirements-driven pass. This is the concrete case that motivated the new Angle E callout above — a matrix walk done ONCE, before shipping the new frame types, would have surfaced most of this list in one pass instead of a dozen. |

**Process lesson (why this PR generated an unusually large number of separate
external-review rounds, not just individual bug classes):** every row above
traces back to ONE of three gaps, now all closed in `/vreview` SKILL.md: (1)
Angle E's mandatory matrix-walk-on-new-area instruction existed but had no
explicit trigger naming "a brand-new frame TYPE is definitionally uncovered" —
added directly to Angle E. (2) Phase 0 step 7's sibling search had no bullet
naming "a different BACKEND behind the same build flag" as a sibling category,
despite this exact codebase routinely shipping 2-4 backends per capability —
added directly to step 7, alongside "every other consumer of a changed default."
(3) Angle H's absence-search had no bullet asking whether a scope-narrowing note
("X is out of scope") was checked clause-by-clause against the RFC section it
was used to dismiss, rather than trusted at face value — added as Angle H's
fourth bullet. **A pattern worth naming on its own:** ~14 rounds of small,
related findings against ONE new subsystem is itself a diagnostic signal, not
just noise to firefight round-by-round — when external review keeps finding
"one more of the same shape" against code that was reviewed in between each
round, stop and ask whether the ORIGINAL implementation ever got a genuine,
requirements-driven Angle E/H pass at all, rather than fixing the Nth instance
and moving on.

Repo: `S:\repo\vlang-http3-server-handshake`, PR vlang/v#28164 (net.quic/net.http Phase 13,
HTTP/3 SERVER role). ~40 findings across ~20 external review rounds, 2026-09-07/08. **The largest
single miss in this corpus, and a different KIND of miss from every row above it:** those are
individual bugs in code that exists; four of these are entire ABSENT SUBSYSTEMS, which no A-I
angle can find because an absent subsystem produces no diff line.

| Class | Where | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| Loss recovery/retransmission absent for the entire server role (4x P1) | `conn.v:2029`/`:2064`/`:1837`, `h3_server_d_http3.v:1061` | NEW: `/vreview` Phase 0 step 8 missing-subsystem checklist + `quic_conformance_matrix.md`'s new Server-role section | ServerHello, handshake CRYPTO, HANDSHAKE_DONE and response STREAM bytes were each sent exactly once and never retained for retransmission (RFC 9000 §13.3). The send path was built and reviewed repeatedly without ever asking "what happens when this datagram is lost?" -- in a protocol whose premise is userspace loss recovery. The client role never exercised this in v1, so the port inherited none of it. |
| Anti-amplification limiter written but never wired (3x P1) | `conn.v:2665`, `accept.v:186` | NEW: step 8's "wired-not-just-written" CALL-SITE grep | `AntiAmplificationLimiter` existed since Phase 13b and no send path ever called it; close packets were unaccounted and the cap was never lifted after Retry validated the address. **A definition grep says "implemented"; only a call-site grep says "reached."** This is Angle G8's own shape (a verification step no call site reaches) recurring far outside crypto -- G8 was scoped to hand-rolled TLS, so nothing generalized it. |
| Unbounded peer-controlled buffering, 4 paths (4x P1) | `h3_frame.v:428`, `h3_conn.v:688`, `h3_server_d_http3.v:191` (x2) | `detect_unbounded_accum.sh` -- existed, was never run against the server files | Incomplete HEADERS, incomplete DATA (aggregate), per-frame and aggregate request-body caps. All four were in the detector's own bug class. Second time this exact detector missed h3 code (see the #28129 `dead_request_streams` row above, where it was scoped `^h2_.*\.v`). **Lesson repeated: a detector that exists but is not RUN is worth nothing, and "I reviewed those files" is not the same claim as "I ran the suite on them."** |
| Role-asymmetric HTTP/3 frame semantics inherited unchanged from the client port (5x P2) | `h3_conn.v:708`, `h3_control_stream.v:52`/`:67` | NEW: step 8's role-swap semantics table | MAX_PUSH_ID must be ACCEPTED as a server (rejected as a client), repeated values allowed when non-decreasing, client PUSH_PROMISE is a connection error, and GOAWAY's identifier is a Push ID server-side vs a Stream ID client-side and must not increase. Each was ported verbatim from working client code, which is exactly why it looked correct: it WAS correct -- for the other role. |
| HTTP server-boundary semantics `h2_server.v` already had (7x) | `h3_server_d_http3.v` various | Phase 0 step 7 sibling search, never run function-by-function against `h2_server.v` | Unknown method silently coerced to GET, CONNECT with empty `:authority`, CONNECT accepted without tunnel dispatch, handler-produced 1xx terminal responses, DATA sent on HEAD/204/304, unvalidated request trailers, oversized streams not kept rejected. The sibling implementing all seven was one file away in the same module. |
| Driver-loop either/or starves timers | `h3_server_d_http3.v:460` | none -- read the loop | `if n > 0 { poll } else { process_timeouts }`: under sustained traffic every iteration takes the datagram branch and due timers never fire. Written, reviewed, and shipped by me; a single-sentence contract re-derivation of the loop ("what must happen every iteration regardless of input?") catches it. |
| TE forwarded in HTTP/3 RESPONSE header + trailer sections (RFC 9114 §4.2: TE is request-only; a response TE is a connection-specific field) -- 2026-09-19, medvednikov pullrequestreview-5246850132 | `h3_server_d_http3.v` `h3_outbound_response_fields`/`h3_outbound_trailer_fields` | Matrix rows added: `quic_conformance_matrix.md` "Outbound response field sections (RFC 9114 §4.2)"; regression test `test_h3_server_drops_te_from_response_headers_and_trailers` (fails on `29a933e6c3`, passes on `e041801019`) | Both filters checked only `h2_conn_specific_headers`, whose own doc comment says TE "is handled on the send side" -- a claim no send-side code honored. **Existing rule:** Angle E matrix walk (no §4.2 field rows existed at all) + Phase 0 step 7 sibling parity (`h2_request_field_error` handles TE on the request side; the response side had no equivalent). **Why it failed:** `/vreview` was never run on the fix commit at all (skipped as "40 lines, checked inline"), and on the earlier server-role pass the §4.2 requirement was never enumerated -- the const's doc comment was trusted as evidence. **New enforcement:** the matrix rows above (walk BOTH sections per row) + the regression test; plus the client-role sibling GAP logged there (`h3_mux_conn_d_http3.v` `wait_response` validates only pseudo-headers -- `h2_mux_conn.v:1686` sibling runs `h2_response_field_error`). Same session: the 9/16 Mac `sync_skills.sh` run had silently REVERTED the whole 2026-09-08 server-role matrix section (newer-mtime-wins, empty manifests) -- restored from the scratchpad extract `serverrole.md`. **HTTP/2 sibling (same gap in `h2_server.v` `send_response`/`h2_outbound_trailer_fields`) fixed and MERGED as vlang/v#28770 (`f59b886d25`, 2026-09-22).** |

**Root cause, one sentence:** the server role was treated as an increment on
working client code rather than as a new implementation needing its own
requirements derivation, so no requirements-driven pass was ever run against
it and only diff-shaped defects could be found.

**Fixes (all landed 2026-09-08):** `/vreview` Phase 0 gained **step 8, the
new-role audit** -- role-swap semantics table, missing-subsystem checklist,
and wired-not-just-written call-site greps, with this PR as the worked
example; the Phase 0 checklist gained a matching line so it cannot be
silently skipped; and `quic_conformance_matrix.md` gained a **Server role
(Phase 13)** section carrying every row above, explicitly labelled a
post-mortem reconstruction (a floor, not a ceiling) with an
explicit "still UNAUDITED" list so no future reader mistakes the fixed rows
for general coverage.

**The process lesson that generalizes past QUIC, and the one worth
re-reading:** ~20 rounds of round-by-round patching happened before anyone
asked why so many findings were landing. **Treat >5 external findings in a
single round as a stop signal** -- it means the requirements pass never
happened, and continuing to patch individual findings cannot converge,
because each round only samples the subset the reviewer happened to look at
that time. Now written into step 8 as an explicit halt condition.

**Related:** [[feedback-code-review-inline]], [[always-code-review-before-commit]],
[[proactive-review-checklist]], [[vreview-skill-data-loss-2026-09-07]]

## vlang/v#28507 — crypto.ecdsa new_key_from_seed on mbedTLS (2026-09-11)

Reviewed commit `39f56879ea`; reviewer JalonSolov ("Local AI", issuecomment-5639045500).

| Class | Reviewed commit | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
