| Backend-sibling parity of a function the diff USES but did not TOUCH (`PrivateKey.bytes()` sized a `.fixed` key from `BN_num_bits`, minimal length, on OpenSSL; curve-width on mbedTLS) | `39f56879ea` `ecdsa_d_use_openssl.v:239` | NEW `detect_ecdsa_fixed_bytes_minimal_len.sh` (manifest `^ecdsa_(d\|notd)_use_openssl\.v`) + regression test `test_new_key_from_seed_fixed_size_leading_zero_seed_keeps_width` | `bash detect_ecdsa_fixed_bytes_minimal_len.sh vlib/crypto/ecdsa/ecdsa_d_use_openssl.v 39f56879ea` → exit 1; working tree → 0. Test: `got 30` bytes on P-256 with old bytes(); passes after `44d01f4af4`. ~1/2 of P-521 keys failed `fixed_size: true` reload under `-d use_openssl`. |

- **Existing rule that should have caught it:** Phase 0 step 7, "the sibling can be a build-flag-selected BACKEND of the same interface" — the review's own sibling table compared `new_key_from_seed` mbedTLS↔OpenSSL, but never `bytes()`, the function whose output the new round-trip test feeds back in.
- **Why it failed:** scope of the sibling check was "functions this diff added", not "every function the new code path CALLS on both backends". Also: the OpenSSL suite was declared "not run — no OpenSSL headers on this box" under negative evidence, when a WSL checkout with OpenSSL (`~/vlang`) existed the whole time — a declared exclusion was treated as sufficient instead of as a to-do.
- **New enforcement mechanism:** detector above; plus rule: for any `crypto.ecdsa`/`net.ssl` change, the negative-evidence line "OpenSSL not run" is NOT acceptable on this machine — run `-d use_openssl` in the WSL worktree (`~/vlang-fix-*`, `thirdparty/tcc` symlinked from `~/vlang`) before committing. Recorded in [[vlang-vreview-before-commit-gate]] (S-- project memory).
- **Second finding, self-caught in the Regression-mode re-review:** the fix itself hand-rolled `(EVP_PKEY_get_bits+7)/8` while `evp_key_size()` (util_d_use_openssl.v:226) already existed — sibling-search-before-writing (CLAUDE.md) applies to fixes too.

| Hand-rolled validator weaker than the authoritative one: rune-literal UTF-8 merge checked only that continuation bytes are 0x80..0xBF, so overlong (`E0 80 80`, `F0 80 80 80`), surrogate (`ED A0 80`) and >U+10FFFF (`F4 90 80 80`) sequences were accepted as one character | `9c4c196d12` `vlib/v/scanner/scanner.v:305` (vlang/v#28882, JalonSolov "Local AI Findings" P1) | Regression test `test_char_literal_byte_escapes_are_one_character_only_when_well_formed_utf8` (scanner_test.v) — every lead C0..F7 x every second-byte boundary x valid/invalid last byte, hex + octal, oracle = `encoding.utf8.validate_str` | fails on `9c4c196d12` (`\xe0\x80\x80`); partial fixes without the min / surrogate / max / continuation check each fail on their own class |

- **Existing rule that should have caught it:** `/vreview` Phase 0 step 7, "the sibling can live in another LANGUAGE or LAYER — find the existing authoritative parser and mirror it exactly; a hand-rolled weaker approximation WILL disagree on some input" (authoritative here: `encoding.utf8.validate` / Unicode Table 3-7). Also CLAUDE.md "test-first for the invariant": the original tests were five hand-picked examples (`\xc0\x80` was the only overlong case), not the rule.
- **Why it failed:** present-but-not-run — `/vreview` was never run on `9c4c196d12` at all (committed straight after the Phase-R test for the reported docs failure), so step 7's sibling search for a UTF-8 validator never happened. The PR description then claimed "overlong and invalid sequences are still rejected" from the one `C0` example.
- **New enforcement mechanism:** the oracle-backed property test above (mechanical; runs in CI via `vlib/v/scanner`). General rule recorded in S-- memory `feedback-no-skipping-review-rules`: /vreview before EVERY vlang commit, including the first commit of a Phase-R fix.
- **Second miss found in the same round, not by the reviewer:** tool-call parameters decode JSON escapes, so a `\u0061` typed into a Bash heredoc / Edit / Write parameter reached the file as a plain `a`. The committed accept-list test silently lost its only `\u` case, and the posted issue #28880 said `` `a` `` for `` `\u0061` ``. Mechanism: grep the written bytes (`od -c`) for every backslash escape after writing V source or posting text through a tool; generate escape-heavy content with `chr(92)`.

## vlang/v#28914 — Windows Boehm abort handler printed after a chained handler that may not return (2026-09-24)

Reviewed commit `47d8482248`; reviewer medvednikov (pullrequestreview-5309842842, P2).

| Class | Reviewed commit | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| Side effect placed after a chained call that may not return: `internal_gc_abort_to_stderr` (`vlib/builtin/builtin_d_gcboehm.c.v`) called Boehm's saved `GC_default_on_abort(NULL)` before printing; with `GC_LOOP_ON_ABORT` set that call spins forever (`thirdparty/libgc/gc.c:27419-27429`), so the fatal message never reached stderr | `47d8482248` | Angle A checklist rule (rev 61); no detector, since whether the chained callee returns lives in its own source, often in another language | `test_boehm_abort_is_reported_before_gc_loop_on_abort` in `vlib/v/tests/gc_boehm_abort_reports_to_stderr_test.v`: fails on `47d8482248` with tcc (empty stderr) and with gcc (only Boehm's pointer-suffixed line), passes on `bbb65c23b2` with both, and on Linux |

- **Existing rule that should have caught it:** `/vreview` Phase 0 step 7, "the sibling can live in another LANGUAGE or LAYER": the handler replaces Boehm's own `GC_default_on_abort`, which prints first and loops last; a line-by-line parity diff against it shows the reversed order.
- **Why it failed:** not known whether `/vreview` ran on `47d8482248` (committed in another session). The code's own comment already said the default handler "honours GC_LOOP_ON_ABORT", so the non-returning path was known; its effect on the statement order was not traced.
- **New enforcement mechanism:** Angle A bullet "Side effect after a chained call that may not return" in both SKILL.md copies and the HTML reference (rev 61).
- **Second miss in the same round (process, not code):** a WSL command `ls -d X && echo EXISTS || git worktree add ...` followed by `git apply` and a rebuild wrote into another session's same-named worktree (`~/wt-28896`); reverted with `git apply -R`. Recorded as S-- memory `wsl-worktree-exists-must-abort`.

## vlang/v#30278 — makev.bat smoke test compiled with the default C compiler, not the toolchain that built the compiler (2026-10-15)

Reviewed commit `531110823f`; reviewer medvednikov (pullrequestreview-5531629970, comment 4281799844).

| Class | Reviewed commit | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| Verification gate diverges from the build it validates: `:smoke_test_updated` (`makev.bat:684`) compiled `examples/hello_world.v` with no `-cc`, while every build of `v_up.exe` passed one (`-cc "!tcc_exe!"`, `-cc msvc`, ...). The V3 driver's implicit C compiler on Windows is the bundled TCC, then a system TCC, then gcc — never MSVC — so a `makev.bat -msvc --local` clone (`--local` skips `download_tcc`, so no bundled TCC) builds a working compiler with `-cc msvc` and then refuses to install it: the smoke compile fails its C build, falls back to the V1 compiler with `retrying with` / `could not build` in the log, and `smoke_log_is_clean` rejects it. Regression against master, which had no smoke test. | `531110823f` | Regression test `test_install_smoke_tests_with_the_toolchain_that_built_the_compiler` in `cmd/tools/makev_test.py` (`python cmd/tools/makev_test.py -k smoke_tests_with_the_toolchain`): runs each of the five install routes (`tcc32_strap`, `bootstrap_self_hosted_fallback`, `clang_strap`, `gcc_strap`, `msvc_strap`) with the compiler stubs recording `-cc`/`-cflags`, and the stub for the new compiler failing any compile that does not name the toolchain that built it | 5/5 subtests fail on `531110823f` ("Smoke test failed; v_up.exe was not installed"), 5/5 pass once `updated_vflags` is set beside every build and reused by the smoke compile. Empirical: with `thirdparty/tcc` renamed away and gcc/clang off PATH, `v.exe -o x examples/hello_world.v` fails its C build (`os: failed to find executable cc`) and falls back to V1 with exit 0, while `v.exe -cc msvc -o x examples/hello_world.v` builds and runs |

- **Existing rule that should have caught it:** `/vreview` Phase 0 step 7, "the sibling can also be every OTHER CONSUMER of a config field": the toolchain selection (`-cc ...`) is consumed by the build line and, as of this PR, by the smoke compile one routine away; a sibling-parity row for `:smoke_test_updated` against the build line it validates shows the dropped flags on one read.
- **Why it failed:** scope narrower than this instance. The rule's wording is about a shared struct field whose DEFAULT changed; nothing in step 7 names a verification/gate step as a sibling of the build it approves, so the new smoke routine got no parity row (its only textual analog in the file, `"%V_UPDATED%" version`, takes no flags). The branch's earlier `/vreview` passes compared the new self-hosted fallback routine against `build_fresh_v_with_tcc` and stopped there.
- **New enforcement mechanism:** Phase 0 step 7 paragraph "The sibling can be the VERIFICATION step of the artifact it checks" (both SKILL.md copies), plus the regression test above, which fails whenever any of the five install routes stops passing its toolchain to the smoke compile.
- **Verification note:** the `-msvc --local` route could not be run end to end on this machine: with the bundled TCC hidden, the MSVC build of `cmd/v` itself fails on Windows Kits ADK header redefinitions (`C2375: 'CreatePipe': redefinition; different linkage`) before the smoke test is reached, an environment issue unrelated to the change. The branch compiler built here does pass `-cc msvc -o x examples/hello_world.v`, which is the smoke compile the fixed route runs. `code-review-reference.html` (now at `S:\Code Review\`, hook and memories repointed) synced as rev 63; corpus row added to `code-review-misses-data.js`.

## vlang/v#30672 — fastc entry renamed to `wmain` at its definition only; references still resolved to `main` (2026-10-15)

Reviewed commit `d2c3084ada`; reviewer medvednikov (pullrequestreview-5541265161, comment 4289405540).

| Class | Reviewed commit | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| Renamed emitted symbol, references not renamed: `parse_function` (`vlib/v/gen/fastc/fn.v:920`) special-cased `is_main` to emit `int wmain(void)` on Windows, but every reference to the V `main` (`fn again() { main() }`, `f := main`) still went through `c_function_name_for_key('main')` → `fastc_c_function_name_for_key` → `main`, so the Windows C had a call to an undefined `main` inside `wmain` | `d2c3084ada` | Angle C checklist rule "a renamed EMITTED symbol has callers too" (rev 64); no grep detector, since the reference renderers are spread over a dozen files and keyed by V function key, not by C spelling | `vlib/v/gen/fastc/windows_entry_call_test.v`: `test_main_references_resolve_to_the_windows_entry_name` fails on `d2c3084ada` (`\twmain();` absent, `main();` emitted inside `wmain`), passes on `a7657b1478`, where `c_function_name_or` resolves key `main` (outside test builds) to `fastc_c_main_name`; `test_main_stays_ordinary_in_test_builds` guards the test-mode exemption |

- **Existing rule that should have caught it:** `/vreview` Angle C, "for each changed function, grep for its callers". Also `/codex-respond` Phase 4 "fix at the right altitude": the name belonged in the one resolution funnel (`c_function_name_or`), not in the definition's branch.
- **Why it failed:** scope narrower than this instance. Angle C was run — it traced the V callers of `fastc_synthesized_main` (one) and of `fastc_c_main_name` (tests) — but its wording is about callers of the changed V function; here the changed thing was the C NAME a definition emits, whose "callers" are every rendering path that spells that name (`c_function_name_for_key`, `&name` function values, prototypes). No step asked "who else produces the string `main`". The V checker forbids calling `main` in ordinary programs, which made the gap easy to dismiss, but fastc does not run the checker and function values of `main` are not rejected.
- **New enforcement mechanism:** Angle C bullet "A renamed EMITTED symbol has callers too" (SKILL.md, all copies) with the fix shape (one resolver for definition and references), plus the regression test above, which fails whenever definition and reference resolution diverge for the entry on any target.

## MSVC-ABI clang gate: one flag source ignored, gate ahead of non-building modes (2026-10-17)

Reviewed commit `09eaae63cd`; reviewer medvednikov (pullrequestreview-5556067468, comments 4301298046, 4301298053), vlang/v#30687.

| Class | Reviewed commit | Detector / check | Repro |
|---|---|---|---|
| (1) Incomplete input enumeration: the retarget exemption read `user_c_flags` (`-cflags`) but not `environment_c_flags` (`CFLAGS`), although every compile-arg builder in `driver.v` starts from `environment_c_flags.clone()` and appends the user flags. (2) Gate altitude: the rejection sat right after compiler selection, before `-print-v-files`/`-print-watched-files` return, so source listings were refused for a compiler they never run | `09eaae63cd` | (1) Phase 0 step 7 "the sibling can be every OTHER CONSUMER of a config field" — enumerate every source that feeds the same decision; (2) Angle D guard completeness — derive a gate's exemption list from the code's actual early returns, or place the gate where the guarded operation happens (sibling: `msvc_require_cl`, which runs only once a C build is required) | `vlib/v/driver/windows_clang_msvc_abi_test.v`: `test_the_rejection_waits_for_the_c_build_and_honours_cflags` (stub clang whose `-dumpmachine` is `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`): fails on `09eaae63cd` at `-print-v-files` (exit 1 with the ABI message), passes once the gate sits beside `msvc_require_cl` and folds `CFLAGS` into the retarget flags |

- **Existing rule that should have caught it:** (1) `/vreview` Phase 0 step 7, consumer-of-config-field sibling search; (2) Angle D "guard completeness" / Angle H "every non-building path exempt".
- **Why it failed:** present-but-not-run. The sibling table compared the new gate with the `-prod` clang picker only; nobody grepped how `-cflags` reaches the compiler (which shows `environment_c_flags` beside it). The exemption list (`c_only`, `output_cross_c`, `check_only`, `only_check_syntax`) was asserted from memory rather than derived by listing every `return` between the gate and the C build.
- **New enforcement mechanism:** the regression test above, plus the fix shape: a "cannot use this C compiler" check belongs next to the existing one (`msvc_require_cl`), at the point the compiler is about to run, so every inspection mode is exempt by construction.

## Rows carried over from code-review-misses-data.js

These reference-table rows were logged only in `code-review-misses-data.js` and had no confident
match above when the corpus moved here. A few may repeat a row above in different words.

| Class | Angle | What was missed | Detector / check |
|---|---|---|---|
| state-machine | E | DATA-after-END_STREAM returned a connection error instead of a per-stream RST_STREAM | State-machine check (Angle E) |
| state-machine | E | DATA frame before HEADERS queued with `headers_done = false`, delivering status 0 to the caller | State-machine check (Angle E) |
| H2PooledTransport.write() reused the shared TLS duration field for its own internal write retry | F | `write()` called the wrapped `ssl.SSLConn`'s `write()`/`write_ptr` without widening `duration` first. Both mbedTLS's and OpenSSL's `write_ptr` reuse that SAME field for their own internal WANT_READ/WANT_WRITE retry loop during the write call — it is not read-specific at the backend level. `new_h2_pooled_transport` leaves it at `h2_pooled_io_timeout` (50ms, needed to bound how long `read()` holds `io_mu`), so a peer that merely stalled mid-write (backpressure, not dead) got a ~50ms failure instead of the intended `h2_pooled_write_stall_limit` (30s). Codex P2 on vlang/v#27643 (pullrequestreview-4628439062, discussion 3522170277). **Reproduction was the hard part:** client sockets in this codebase are never switched to non-blocking (only the server's threaded-accept path does that), and mbedtls's `net_would_block()` is hard-coded to return 0 on a blocking POSIX socket — so `MBEDTLS_ERR_SSL_WANT_WRITE` is structurally unreachable via a normal client dial, no amount of payload/timing tuning could reach it. Confirmed by forcing the socket non-blocking + shrinking `SO_SNDBUF` (both already public APIs) against a captive peer, isolating at the `SSLConn.write_ptr` level directly: `duration=50ms` failed at 65ms, `duration=2s` failed at 2.014s — the failure latency exactly tracks whatever `set_read_timeout()` last set. Fix: widen to `h2_pooled_write_stall_limit` immediately before `conn.write()` (held inside `io_mu`, so no race with `read()`), restore right after, on every exit path. | `detect_pooled_write_duration_reuse.sh`; runtime test `test_h2_pooled_transport_write_survives_stall_past_io_timeout` |
| evict_idle_h2_if_over_cap could evict the connection its own caller just dialed | D | Registering a fresh `h2_conns` entry and immediately running the over-cap eviction scan in the same locked section meant the brand-new connection (`active_streams` still 0 — `do_h2` hasn't run on it yet) looked idle to its own registration's eviction pass. If it happened to be the only idle candidate found (every other pooled connection busy, or the shared budget consumed entirely by h1 idle entries this function never scans), the loop's first-candidate-wins tie-break picked it, tearing the connection down before its own request ever ran. Codex P1 on vlang/v#27643 (pullrequestreview-4628439062, discussion 3522170273). Fix: `evict_idle_h2_if_over_cap(just_registered_key string)` — the scan skips that key explicitly, rather than relying on the (previously believed sufficient, but incorrect once combined with an all-idle map) “newest idle_since always loses ties” assumption. | runtime test `test_h2_pool_does_not_evict_its_own_new_connection` |
| read-timeout watchdog armed before the request body finished uploading | A | The read-timeout watchdog (added the round before, see the “lingers for the full deadline” row above) was spawned in `do()`, before HEADERS/body ever reached the wire — so it could fire while still waiting on `wmu` behind another writer, or mid-upload on a large POST, aborting a request that had not even started waiting for a response yet. `req.read_timeout` documents itself as the response-read deadline, not an overall request deadline. Codex P1 on vlang/v#27643 (pullrequestreview-4628439062, discussion 3522170275). Fix: moved the `spawn` into `do_on_stream`, right before `wait_response` — once the request is fully sent and only the response wait remains. | runtime test `test_mux_read_timeout_excludes_upload_blocked_phase` |
| excluding uploads from read_timeout left the upload phase with no timeout bound at all | C | The fix directly above (arm the watchdog only after upload completes, so `read_timeout` can't fire mid-upload) has a side effect its own Codex finding didn't ask about: `send_body_on_stream`'s flow-control wait (`c.fcv.wait()`) has no timeout of its own — it relies entirely on the connection dying or the watchdog calling `wake_send()`. With the watchdog now spawned only AFTER upload, a peer that drains to the initial window and then simply never grants another byte of credit (never RSTs, never GOAWAYs, just goes quiet) pins the stream, the pooled connection (`active_streams` never reaches 0, so it's never idle-evicted either), and the caller's thread — forever. Self-caught during `/vreview` on the fix above, before push (not an external finding); found via the Angle C sync-vs-mux parity check: `H2Conn.send_body` (the one-shot path) waits for window via `c.next_frame()!`, a blocking read bounded by the connection's own read timeout, so the sync path never had this gap — only the newly “fixed” mux path did. Fix: split the single watchdog into two independent phase-scoped calls, each with its own budget — upload bounded by a NEW, decoupled `h2_stream_upload_stall_limit` (5min, not tied to `req.read_timeout` so it can never reintroduce the original bug; `-d h2_stream_upload_stall_limit_ms=<n>` overridable for a fast test, mirroring `mbedtls_client_read_timeout_ms`'s `$d()` pattern), response-wait still bounded by `req.read_timeout` as before. **Lesson: excluding a phase from a shared timeout to fix a false-positive can silently remove the only bound that phase had — check what ELSE relied on the timeout you just narrowed before calling the narrowing complete.** | no detector (timing-shaped, not statically visible); runtime test `test_mux_upload_permanently_stalled_eventually_times_out` (skips without the `-d` override so the default suite stays fast) |
| evict_idle_h2_if_over_cap's own fix (round 2) was itself incomplete: cap check counted both pools, eviction loop scanned only one | F | Round 2 fixed “h2_conns has no eviction at all” by adding `evict_idle_h2_if_over_cap`, whose cap CHECK correctly counted both pools (`total_idle_locked() + h2_conns.len <= max_idle_conns`) — but its eviction LOOP only ever scanned `h2_conns`. With `max_idle_conns` filled entirely by an idle h1 connection and no other h2 candidate (the new dial's own entry is excluded from candidacy), NEITHER the new h2 connection nor the h1 entry was ever freed, silently exceeding the documented global cap indefinitely. Codex P2 on vlang/v#27643 round 4 (pullrequestreview-4630174759). Missed by BOTH round 2's and round 3's `/vreview` passes (round 3 touched the same function to add the just-registered-key exclusion and still didn't re-derive its full contract) — this is the concrete case for the “repeat-offender function” rule added to `/vreview` Phase 0 as a direct result: a function on its second+ touch this branch needs a from-scratch contract re-derivation, not another diff-only scan. Fix: unified the two separate, protocol-scoped evictors into one `evict_oldest_idle_locked` that scans BOTH pools and evicts whichever is truly oldest-idle. **Sibling instance found by the new detector, open through round 4, fixed round 5:** `checkout()`'s `idle_timeout` expiry had no `h2_conns` equivalent at all — resolved via a separate `H2MuxConn.is_idle_expired` check in `h2_round_trip`'s own fast path (see the round-5 row below), not by adding h2 handling to `checkout()` itself (which stays h1-only by design). | `detect_pool_cap_scope_symmetry.sh`; runtime test `test_h2_pool_evicts_h1_idle_to_make_room_for_h2` |
| V compiler bug: two same-signature closures in one function conflate their capture context | A | Not a Codex finding — a genuine V compiler defect found while implementing the round-4 fix for the read-timeout progress-reset. `do_on_stream` spawns `h2_stream_timeout_watchdog` twice (upload phase, response phase); adding a second closure argument (`activity fn [s] () time.Time {...}`) alongside the existing `done fn [s] () bool {...}` at BOTH call sites meant two pairs of identically-shaped closure literals existed in one enclosing function. The response phase's `activity` closure silently returned only the connection's INITIAL last-activity snapshot from spawn time, completely defeating the fix, with no compile error and no obviously-wrong behavior short of measuring actual elapsed time against expectation. A `voidptr()` cast test is a RED HERRING here — it fails identically for a correctly-working closure (verified via a minimal repro), so it does not distinguish the bug from correct behavior. Diagnosed only via a from-scratch minimal, fully isolated repro (no other test infrastructure to obscure timing) reproducing the exact two-same-signature-closures shape. Fix: pass the captured stream as an explicit parameter to plain, non-capturing NAMED top-level functions instead of closures — sidesteps the conflation entirely. | no detector (V compiler behavior, not project code); manual empirical repro method documented in the Angle A bullet and in memory (v-closure-capture-conflation-bug.md) |
| sibling connection-ID transport parameters missing the SAME 20-byte limit | E | **Found by this session's OWN sibling sweep, not Codex** — after fixing the preferred_address instance above, auditing the other connection-ID-carrying fields in the SAME file (`initial_source_connection_id`, `original_destination_connection_id`, `retry_source_connection_id` — each defined as an echo of a wire DCID/SCID already bound by the identical limit, RFC 9000 §7.3, verified via a live RFC fetch) found NONE of them had the check either, on either encode or decode. Closed proactively in the same session rather than waiting for a fifth Codex round to find the 2nd/3rd/4th instances one at a time. | 6 new tests, one per field per direction |
| Self-caught: fixing the row above discarded its own new error code across a caller boundary | C | `process_certificate_or_request` unconditionally remapped ANY `parse_certificate` failure to a generic `decode_error`, discarding the new `unsupported_extension` classification above — the exact `err.code() != 0` preservation pattern already used by 3 OTHER call sites in the SAME file, just never applied to this fourth one. Reproduced: driving a real handshake to a tampered Certificate and checking `err.code()` returned decode_error's code, not the expected one. **Not a Codex finding — caught by this round's own /vreview pass before push.** | tls13_handshake_test.v test_process_certificate_or_request_preserves_illegal_extension_code |
| ALPN protocol names required to be unique — REFUTED as an RFC requirement, implemented anyway on maintainer request | E | Fetched RFC 7301 §3.1 directly: it forbids empty/truncated strings but states no uniqueness requirement anywhere. `protocols` is caller-supplied (a duplicate is reachable) but creates no wire-format or protocol ambiguity — kept off the baseline in round 7, no code change at the time. Since `module quic` compiles as ONE translation unit, any ungated file hard-fails `docker-ubuntu-musl` CI (`fatal error: sys/cdefs.h: No such file or directory`) via `tls13_handshake.v`’s transitive `crypto.ecdsa` OpenSSL dependency — caught both times by CI, not review, since nothing enforced the convention mechanically before a diff was pushed. **Fix:** gated the 5 newly-added Phase 8 files (`connection_close_test.v`, `ecn_test.v`, `idle_timeout_test.v`, `pmtu_test.v`, `stateless_reset_test.v`), same pattern as PR #27884’s own fix (commit `39c3f2f414`), verified both directions (SKIP with `PKG_CONFIG_PATH` pointed nowhere; pass with real OpenSSL), and wrote a detector so a THIRD phase cannot repeat this silently. | `detect_quic_test_missing_openssl_gate.sh` |
| scheduler aggregator missing new deadline field | D | `QuicConn.closing_deadline` and `compute_next_timeout()` were both written in the SAME commit (vlang/v#28083 Phase 9), but the aggregator merged `idle_timeout_deadline()`/`loss_detection.next_timeout()` and never `closing_deadline` — a connection reaching `.closing`/`.draining` with nothing else armed returned `next_timeout = none`, so a real caller-driven event loop would never learn to call `process_timeouts()` again despite the RFC 9000 §10.2.2 bound being correctly recorded. **Missed TWICE by two separate reviews of the SAME code**: the original Phase 9 `/vreview` pass never caught it, and a LATER Phase-R fix that added the missing write to `closing_deadline` (a different, narrower reported gap — the field was not being SET at all) never traced the field’s read side, so the aggregator gap survived untouched underneath a fix that looked complete. Caught only by a maintainer’s own separate “Local AI Review” pass. The integration test also masked it: it always had a stale, never-acked PTO timer armed (the fixture never constructs ACK frames back to the client), so `next_timeout` was coincidentally non-none even with the bug present — a same-module white-box test that force-zeroed `bytes_in_flight` was needed to isolate the true “nothing else armed” scenario. **Lesson: when a struct has one function whose job is to merge several optional deadline fields into a single “next time to act” value, ANY new deadline-shaped field added anywhere in that struct is a candidate the aggregator must also merge — grep the struct for every other `?`-typed field and confirm each is referenced in the aggregator, every time either side changes.** | `detect_scheduler_field_coverage.sh` |
| time-unit-scale mismatch (nanoseconds vs milliseconds) | G | Self-caught mid-fix on vlang/v#28083 (net.quic Phase 9): a maintainer “Local AI Review” finding (idle timer never reset on receive) led to discovering `idle_timeout_deadline()` combines a `time.Duration` with `now` via a bare `u64()` cast. Working from an assumption carried over from an EARLIER, ALSO-WRONG fix in the same file (`closing_or_draining_deadline`’s pre-existing `.milliseconds()` call, which looked like established precedent), the ORIGINAL code was “corrected” to ADD `.milliseconds()` — backwards. `now` is actually a `time.sys_mono_now()`-sourced nanosecond instant throughout the module (documented in `IdleTimeoutState.last_reset`’s own doc comment, already read but not weighted correctly). The regression test written to Phase-R this even PASSED against the actually-broken pre-fix code on the first attempt, because its own `now`-delta magnitudes were ALSO chosen under the same wrong millisecond assumption. Settled only by grepping `loss_detection_test.v`’s own pre-existing, already-passing assertions (`assert ld.rtt.latest_rtt == 1000 * time.nanosecond` for a delta of exactly 1000), independent and unambiguous proof of the true convention. **Lesson: verify a module’s time-unit convention via an EXISTING TEST ASSERTION, not by inspecting one sibling function’s conversion call — that sibling can be the first instance of the same mistake, and following it as precedent doubles down instead of catching it.** Fixed both `idle_timeout_deadline()` (reverted to the original, correct form) and `closing_or_draining_deadline` (removed the wrong `.milliseconds()`); sibling-searched the whole module for any other instance, none found. | none (semantic fact, not a syntactic pattern -- see G9) |
